# Fetch
    
Living Standard — Last Updated 2 September 2026  
      

         Participate:     [GitHub whatwg/fetch](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch) ([new issue](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new/choose), [open issues](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues))     [Chat on Matrix](https://whatwg.org/chat)    
Commits:     [GitHub whatwg/fetch/commits](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits)     [Snapshot as of this commit](/commit-snapshots/394d20d144ed1401c2c0e02c35bc3608cb2a2269/ "You can also press the 'y' key")    
Tests:     [web-platform-tests fetch/](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch) ([ongoing work](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch))

    [简体中文](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/)     [한국어](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/) 
         

    

   
## Abstract
   
The Fetch standard defines requests, responses, and the process that binds them: 
fetching.

## Goals
   
The goal is to unify fetching across the web platform and provide consistent handling 
ofeverything that involves, including:
   
    - URL schemes     
- Redirects     
- Cross-origin semantics     
- CSP [[CSP]](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")     
- Fetch Metadata [[FETCH-METADATA]](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers")     
- Service workers [[SW]](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly")     
- Mixed Content [[MIX]](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content")     
- Upgrade Insecure Requests [[UPGRADE-INSECURE-REQUESTS]](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests")     
- \`Referer\` [[REFERRER]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css)   
   
To do so it also supersedes the HTTP \`[h-entry
Origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css "subtitle")\` header semanticsoriginally defined in
*The Web Origin Concept*. [[ORIGIN]](h-entry "subtitle")
   
## 1. Preface
   
At a high level, fetching a resource is a fairly simple operation. A request goes 
in, aresponse comes out. The details of that operation arehowever quite involved 
and used to not be written down carefully and differ from one APIto the next.
   
Numerous APIs provide the ability to fetch a resource, e.g. HTML’s h-entry
img andh-entry
script element, CSS' h-entry
cursor and h-entry
list-style-image,the h-entry
navigator.sendBeacon() and h-entry
self.importScripts() JavaScriptAPIs. The Fetch Standard provides a unified architecture for these features 
so they areall consistent when it comes to various aspects of fetching, such as redirects 
and theCORS protocol.
   
The Fetch Standard also defines the [h-entry
fetch()](h-entry "subtitle") JavaScript API, whichexposes most of the networking functionality at a fairly low level of abstraction.
   
## 2. Infrastructure
   
This specification depends on the Infra Standard. [[INFRA]](h-entry "subtitle")
   
This specification uses terminology from *ABNF*, *Encoding*,*HTML*, *HTTP*, *MIME 
Sniffing*, *Streams*,*URL*, *Web IDL*, *WebSockets*, and *WebTransport*.[[ABNF]](h-entry "subtitle")[[ENCODING]](h-entry "subtitle")[[HTML]](h-entry "subtitle")[[HTTP]](h-entry "subtitle")[[MIMESNIFF]](h-entry "subtitle")[[STREAMS]](h-entry "subtitle")[[URL]](h-entry "subtitle")[[WEBIDL]](h-entry "subtitle")[[WEBSOCKETS]](h-entry "subtitle")[[WEBTRANSPORT]](h-entry "subtitle")
   
*ABNF* means ABNF as augmented by HTTP (in particular the addition of h-entry
#)and RFC 7405. [[RFC7405]](h-entry "subtitle")
   
---
   
*Credentials* are HTTP cookies, TLS client certificates, and [authentication entries](h-entry "subtitle") (for HTTP authentication). [[COOKIES]](h-entry "subtitle")[[TLS]](h-entry "subtitle") [[HTTP]](h-entry "subtitle")
   
---
   
A *fetch params* is a [struct](h-entry "subtitle") used as a bookkeeping detail by the[fetch](h-entry "subtitle") algorithm. It has the following [items](h-entry "subtitle"):
       *request*     A [request](h-entry "subtitle").     *process request body chunk 
length* (default null)     *process request end-of-body* (default null)     *process 
early hints response* (default null)     *process response* (default null)     *process 
response end-of-body* (default null)     *process response consume body* (default 
null)     Null or an algorithm.     *task destination* (default null)     Null, a 
[global object](h-entry "subtitle"), or a [parallel queue](h-entry "subtitle").     *cross-origin 
isolated capability* (default false)     A boolean.     *controller* (default a new 
[fetch controller](h-entry "subtitle"))     A [fetch controller](h-entry "subtitle").     *timing 
info*     A [fetch timing info](h-entry "subtitle").     *preloaded response candidate* 
(default null)     Null, "h-entry
pending", or a [response](h-entry "subtitle").      
A *fetch controller* is a [struct](h-entry "subtitle") used to enable callers of[fetch](h-entry "subtitle") to perform certain operations on it after it has started. It has the following[items](h-entry "subtitle"):
       *state* (default "h-entry
ongoing")     "h-entry
ongoing", "h-entry
terminated", or "h-entry
aborted"     *full timing info* (default null)     Null or a [fetch timing info](h-entry "subtitle").    
*report timing steps* (default null)     Null or an algorithm accepting a [global object](h-entry "subtitle").    
*serialized abort reason* (default null)     Null or a [Record](h-entry "subtitle") (result of [StructuredSerialize](h-entry "subtitle")).    
*next manual redirect steps* (default null)     Null or an algorithm accepting nothing. 
     

    
To *report timing* for a[fetch controller](h-entry "subtitle") controller given a 
[global object](h-entry "subtitle") global:
   

     1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): controller’s [report timing steps](h-entry "subtitle") is non-null. 
     
2.       Call controller’s [report timing steps](h-entry "subtitle") with global.
    

      

    
To *process the next manual redirect* for a[fetch controller](h-entry "subtitle") controller:
   

     1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): controller’s [next manual redirect steps](h-entry "subtitle") is non-null. 
     
2.       Call controller’s [next manual redirect steps](h-entry "subtitle").
    

      

    
To*extract full timing info*given a [fetch controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")
controller:
   

     1.       [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"): controller’s [full timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is non-null. 
     
2.       Return controller’s [full timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").
    

      

    
To *abort* a [fetch controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")controller 
with an optional error:
   

     1.       Set controller’s [state](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to "aborted". 
     
2.       Let fallbackError be an "[AbortError](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")" [DOMException](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Set error to fallbackError if it is not given. 
     
4.       Let serializedError be [StructuredSerialize](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")(error). If that threw an exception, catch it, and let serializedError be [StructuredSerialize](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")(fallbackError). 
     
5.       Set controller’s [serialized abort reason](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to serializedError.
    

      

    
To *deserialize a serialized abort reason*, given null or a [Record](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css
"subtitle")abortReason and a [realm](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") realm:
   

     1.       Let fallbackError be an "[AbortError](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")" [DOMException](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let deserializedError be fallbackError. 
     
3.       If abortReason is non-null, then set deserializedError to [StructuredDeserialize](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")(abortReason, realm). If that threw an exception or returned undefined, then set deserializedError to fallbackError. 
     
4.       Return deserializedError.
    

      

    
To *terminate* a [fetch controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")controller, 
set controller’s [state](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to"h-entry status-LS
terminated".
      
A [fetch params](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") fetchParams 
is *aborted* ifits [controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [state](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is"h-entry status-LS
aborted".
   
A [fetch params](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") fetchParams 
is *canceled* ifits [controller](# "subtitle")’s [state](# "subtitle") is"h-entry status-LS
aborted" or "h-entry status-LS
terminated".
   
A *fetch timing info* is a [struct](# "subtitle") used to maintain timinginformation needed by
*Resource Timing* and *Navigation Timing*. It has thefollowing [items](# "subtitle"): [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](# "subtitle") [[NAVIGATION-TIMING]](# "subtitle")
       *start time* (default 0)     *redirect start time* (default 0)     *redirect 
end time* (default 0)     *post-redirect start time* (default 0)     *final service 
worker start time* (default 0)     *final network-request start time* (default 0) 
    *first interim network-response start time* (default 0)     *final network-response 
start time* (default 0)     *end time* (default 0)     A h-entry status-LS
[DOMHighResTimeStamp](# "subtitle").     *final connection timing info* (default null)     Null or a 
[connection timing info](# "subtitle").     *service worker timing info* (default 
null)     Null or a [service worker timing info](# "subtitle").     *server-timing 
headers* (default « »)     A [list](# "subtitle") of strings.     *render-blocking* 
(default false)     A boolean.      
A *response body info* is a [struct](# "subtitle") used to maintaininformation needed by *Resource 
Timing* and *Navigation Timing*. It has thefollowing [items](# "subtitle"): [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](# "subtitle") [[NAVIGATION-TIMING]](# "subtitle")
       *encoded size* (default 0)     *decoded size* (default 0)     A number.   
  *content type* (default the empty string)     An [ASCII string](# "subtitle").     *content 
encoding* (default the empty string)     An [ASCII string](# "subtitle").      

    
To*create an opaque timing info*,given a [fetch timing info](# "subtitle") timingInfo, 
return a new[fetch timing info](# "subtitle") whose [start time](# "subtitle") and[post-redirect start time](# "subtitle") are
timingInfo’s[start time](# "subtitle"), [end time](# "subtitle") istimingInfo’s 
[end time](# "subtitle"), and[render-blocking](# "subtitle") is timingInfo’s[render-blocking](# "subtitle").
      

    
To *queue a fetch task*, given an algorithm algorithm, a[global object](# "subtitle") or a [parallel queue](# "subtitle")
taskDestination, run thesesteps:
   

     1.       If taskDestination is a [parallel queue](# "subtitle"), then [enqueue](# "subtitle") algorithm to taskDestination. 
     
2.       Otherwise, [queue a global task](# "subtitle") on the [networking task source](# "subtitle") with taskDestination and algorithm.
    

      

    
To check if the [environment settings object](# "subtitle") environment*is offline*:
    
     -       If the user agent assumes it does not have internet connectivity, then return true. 
     
-       Return environment’s [WebDriver BiDi network is offline](# "subtitle").
    
      
---
   
To *serialize an integer*, represent it as a string of the shortest possible decimalnumber.
   
This will be replaced by a more descriptive algorithm in Infra. See[infra/201](# "subtitle").
   
### 2.1. URL
   
A *local scheme* is "h-entry status-LS
about", "h-entry status-LS
blob", or"h-entry status-LS
data".
   
A [URL](# "subtitle") *is local* if its [scheme](# "subtitle") is a[local scheme](# "subtitle").
   
This definition is also used by *Referrer Policy*. [[REFERRER]](# "subtitle")
   
An *HTTP(S) scheme* is "h-entry status-LS
http" or"h-entry status-LS
https".
   
A *fetch scheme* is "h-entry status-LS
about", "h-entry status-LS
blob","h-entry status-LS
data", "h-entry status-LS
file", or an [HTTP(S) scheme](# "subtitle").
   
[HTTP(S) scheme](# "subtitle") and [fetch scheme](# "subtitle") are also used by *HTML*.[[HTML]](# "subtitle")
   
### 2.2. HTTP
   
While [fetching](# "subtitle") encompasses more than just HTTP, itborrows a number of concepts from HTTP and applies these to resources obtained via othermeans (e.g., h-entry status-LS
data URLs).
   
An *HTTP tab or space* is U+0009 TAB or U+0020 SPACE.
   
*HTTP whitespace* is U+000A LF, U+000D CR, or an [HTTP tab or space](# "subtitle").
   
[HTTP whitespace](# "subtitle") is only useful for specific constructs that are reused outsidethe context of HTTP headers (e.g., [MIME types](# "subtitle")). For HTTP header values, using[HTTP tab or space](# "subtitle") is preferred, and outside that context [ASCII whitespace](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") ispreferred. Unlike [ASCII whitespace](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") this excludes U+000C FF.
   
An *HTTP newline byte* is 0x0A (LF) or 0x0D (CR).
   
An *HTTP tab or space byte* is 0x09 (HT) or 0x20 (SP).
   
An *HTTP whitespace byte* is an [HTTP newline byte](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") or[HTTP tab or space byte](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").
   

    
To*collect an HTTP quoted string*from a [string](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")
input, given a [position variable](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") positionand 
an optional boolean extract-value (default false):
   

     1.       Let positionStart be position. 
     
2.       Let value be the empty string. 
     
3.       [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"): the [code point](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") at position within input is U+0022 ("). 
     
4.       Advance position by 1. 
     
5.       While true:  
     

       1.         Append the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") that are not U+0022 (")   or U+005C (\\) from input, given position, to value.   
       
2.         If position is past the end of input, then   [break](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").   
       
3.         Let quoteOrBackslash be the [code point](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") at position within   input.   
       
4.         Advance position by 1.   
       
5.         If quoteOrBackslash is U+005C (\\), then:    
       

         1.           If position is past the end of input, then append U+005C (\\) to     value and [break](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").     
         
2.           Append the [code point](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") at position within input to     value.     
         
3.           Advance position by 1.    
        

       
-         Otherwise:    
       

         1.           [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"): quoteOrBackslash is U+0022 (").     
         
2.           [Break](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").    
        

      

     
-       If extract-value is true, then return value. 
     
-       Return the [code points](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") from positionStart to position, inclusive, within input.
    

    

           
|                     |
||--------------------|--------|---------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| -                   | -                  | -      | -                                     |
|                     | "h-entry status-LS |
| "\"                 | "h-entry status-LS |
| "\"                 | "h-entry status-LS |
| \"                  | 2                  |
|                     | "h-entry status-LS |
| "Hello" World"      | "h-entry status-LS |
| "Hello""            | "h-entry status-LS |
| Hello"              | 7                  |
|                     | "h-entry status-LS |
| "Hello \\ World\""" | "h-entry status-LS |
| "Hello \\ World\""" | "h-entry status-LS |
| Hello \ World""     | 18                 |
     
The [position variable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") always starts at 0 in these examples.
          
#### 2.2.1. Methods
   
A *method* is a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that matches the[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") token production.
   
A *CORS-safelisted method* is a[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is \`h-entry status-LS
GET\`,\`h-entry status-LS
HEAD\`, or \`h-entry status-LS
POST\`.
   
A *forbidden method* is a [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") match for \`h-entry status-LS
CONNECT\`,\`h-entry status-LS
TRACE\`, or \`h-entry status-LS
TRACK\`.[[HTTPVERBSEC1]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [[HTTPVERBSEC2]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [[HTTPVERBSEC3]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
To *normalize* a[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), if it is a [byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")match for \`h-entry status-LS
DELETE\`, \`h-entry status-LS
GET\`,\`h-entry status-LS
HEAD\`, \`h-entry status-LS
OPTIONS\`, \`h-entry status-LS
POST\`, or\`h-entry status-LS
PUT\`, [byte-uppercase](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") it.
   
[Normalization](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is done for backwards compatibility andconsistency across APIs as [methods](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are actually "case-sensitive".
   
Using \`h-entry status-LS
patch\` is highly likely to result in a\`h-entry status-LS
405 Method Not Allowed\`. \`h-entry status-LS
PATCH\` is much more likely tosucceed.
   
There are no restrictions on [methods](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). \`h-entry status-LS
CHICKEN\` is perfectlyacceptable (and not a misspelling of \`h-entry status-LS
CHECKIN\`). Other than those that are[normalized](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") there are no casing restrictions either.\`h-entry status-LS
Egg\` or \`h-entry status-LS
eGg\` would be fine, though uppercase is encouraged forconsistency.
   
#### 2.2.2. Headers
   
HTTP generally refers to a header as a "field" or "header field". The web platformuses 
the more colloquial term "header". [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A *header list* is a [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of zero or more[headers](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). It is initially « ».
   
A [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is essentially a specialized multimap: an ordered list ofkey-value pairs with potentially duplicate keys. Since headers other than \`h-entry status-LS
Set-Cookie\`are always combined when exposed to client-side JavaScript, implementations could 
choose a moreefficient representation, as long as they also support an associated 
data structure for\`h-entry status-LS
Set-Cookie\` headers.
   

    
To*get a structured field value*given a [header name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") name 
and a string type from a[header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") list, run these 
steps. They return null or a[structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   

     1.       [Assert](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): type is one of "dictionary", "list", or "item". 
     
2.       Let value be the result of [getting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") name from list. 
     
3.       If value is null, then return null. 
     
4.       Let result be the result of [parsing structured fields](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with input_string set to value and header_type set to type. 
     
5.       If parsing failed, then return null. 
     
6.       Return result.
    

    
[Get a structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") intentionally does not distinguish between a[header](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") not being present and its [value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") failing to parse as a[structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). This ensures uniform processing across the web platform.
      

    
To*set a structured field value*given a [tuple](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") ([header name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
name, [structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")structuredValue), 
in a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") list:
   

     1.       Let serializedValue be the result of executing the [serializing structured fields](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") algorithm on structuredValue. 
     
2.       [Set](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (name, serializedValue) in list.
    

    
[Structured field values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are defined as objects which HTTP can (eventually)serialize in interesting and efficient ways. For the moment, Fetch only supports[header values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") as [byte sequences](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), which means that these objects can be set in[header lists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") only via serialization, and they can be obtained from[header lists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") only by parsing. In the future the fact that they are objects might bepreserved end-to-end. [[RFC9651]](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle")
      
---
   

    
A [header list](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "dt-updated") list*contains* a[header name](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues "You can also press the 'y' key")
name if list [contains](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key") a[header](/commit-snapshots/394d20d144ed1401c2c0e02c35bc3608cb2a2269/ "You can also press the 'y' key") whose [name](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") is a [byte-case-insensitive](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese") match
forname.
      

    
To *get* a [header name](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean")name from a [header list](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean")
list, run these steps. They return nullor a [header value](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3").
   

     1.       If list [does not contain](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") name, then return null. 
     
2.       Return the [values](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") of all [headers](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") in list whose [name](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a [byte-case-insensitive](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for name, separated from each other by 0x2C 0x20, in order.
    

      

    
To*get, decode, and split*a [header name](#bodies "Content Security Policy Level 3") name 
from [header list](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") list, run thesesteps. 
They return null or a [list](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [strings](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3").
   

     1.       Let value be the result of [getting](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") name from list. 
     
2.       If value is null, then return null. 
     
3.       Return the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") value.
    

      

         
This is how [get, decode, and split](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") functions in practice with \`secno
A\` as the name argument: 
    
|                        |
||----------------------------------|--------|
| -                      | -                                |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: nosniff,            |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`nosniff`", "" »            |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: nosniff             |
| B: sniff               |
| A:                     |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A:                     |
| B: sniff               |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "" »                         |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| B: sniff               |
| ```                    |
|                        | null                             |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: text/html;", x/x``` |
|                        | « "`text/html;", x/x`" »       |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: text/html;"         |
| A: x/x                 |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x/x;test="hi",y/y   |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`x/x;test="hi"`", "`y/y`" » |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x/x;test="hi"       |
| C: **bingo**           |
| A: y/y                 |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x / x,,,1           |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`x / x`", "", "", "`1`" »   |
|                        |                                  |
| ```content             |
| A: x / x               |
| A:,                   |
| A: 1                   |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```content             |
| A: "1,2", 3            |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`"1,2"`", "`3`" »           |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: "1,2"               |
| D: 4                   |
| A: 3                   |
| ```                    |
