CSP’s `form-action` needs to be a hook directly in HTML’s navigate or form submission algorithm. 

    
CSP will also need to check [request](# "subtitle")’s [client](# "subtitle")’s [global object](# "subtitle")’s [associated `Document`](# "subtitle")’s [ancestor navigables](# "subtitle") for various CSP directives.
      
---
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*priority*, which is "`high`", "`low`", or"`auto`". 
Unless stated otherwise it is "`auto`".
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*internal priority* (null or an[implementation-defined](# "subtitle") object). Unless otherwise stated it is null.
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*origin*, which is"`client`" or an [origin](# "subtitle"). Unless stated otherwise it
is"`client`".
   
"`client`" is changed to an [origin](# "subtitle") during[fetching](# "subtitle"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](# "subtitle")’s [origin](# "subtitle").
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*top-level navigation initiator origin*, 
which is an [origin](# "subtitle")or null. Unless stated otherwise it is null.
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*policy container*, which is"`client`" or a 
[policy container](# "subtitle"). Unless stated otherwise it is"`client`".
   
"`client`" is changed to a [policy container](# "subtitle") during[fetching](# "subtitle"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](# "subtitle")’s [policy container](# "subtitle").
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*referrer*, which is"`no-referrer`", "`client`", or a [URL](# "subtitle"). Unless stated otherwise itis
"`client`".
   
"`client`" is changed to "`no-referrer`" or a [URL](# "subtitle")during [fetching](# "subtitle"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](# "subtitle")’s [referrer](# "subtitle").
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*referrer policy*, which is a[referrer policy](# "subtitle"). Unless stated otherwise it is the empty string. [[REFERRER]](# "subtitle")
   
This can be used to override the referrer policy to be used for this[request](# "subtitle").
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*mode*, which is"`same-origin`", "`cors`", "`no-cors`","`navigate`", 
"`websocket`", or "`webtransport`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`no-cors`".
   

         "`same-origin`"       Used to ensure requests are made to same-origin URLs. [Fetch](# "subtitle") will return a  [network error](# "subtitle") if the request is not made to a same-origin URL.      
"`cors`"       For requests whose [response tainting](# "subtitle") gets set to "`cors`", 
makes  the request a [CORS request](# "subtitle") — in which case, fetch will return a [network error](# "subtitle") if the  requested resource does not understand the [CORS protocol](# "subtitle"), or if the requested resource is  one that intentionally does not participate in the [CORS protocol](# "subtitle").      
"`no-cors`"       Restricts requests to using [CORS-safelisted methods](# "subtitle") and  [CORS-safelisted request-headers](# "subtitle"). Upon success, fetch will return an  [opaque filtered response](# "subtitle").      
"`navigate`"       This is a special mode used only when [navigating](# "subtitle") between documents.      
"`websocket`"       This is a special mode used only when  [establishing a WebSocket connection](# "subtitle").      
"`webtransport`"       This is a special mode used only by `[WebTransport(url, options)](# "subtitle")`.         
Even though the default [request](# "subtitle") [mode](# "subtitle") is "`no-cors`", standards 
are highly discouraged from using it for new features. It is rather unsafe.
      
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*use-CORS-preflight flag*. Unless statedotherwise, 
it is unset.
   
The [use-CORS-preflight flag](# "subtitle") being set is one of several conditions that resultsin a [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle"). The [use-CORS-preflight flag](# "subtitle") is set if either one or moreevent listeners are registered on an `[XMLHttpRequestUpload](# "subtitle")` 
object or if a `[ReadableStream](# "subtitle")`object is used in a request.
   
A [request](# "subtitle") has an associated*credentials mode*,which is "`omit`", "`same-origin`", 
or"`include`". Unless stated otherwise, it is "`same-origin`".
   

         "`omit`"       Excludes credentials from this request, and causes any credentials 
sent back in the response  to be ignored.       "`same-origin`"       Include credentials with 
requests made to same-origin URLs, and use any credentials sent back  in responses 
from same-origin URLs.       "`include`"       Always includes credentials with this request, 
and always use any credentials sent back in the  response.         
[Request](# "subtitle")’s [credentials mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") controls the flow of [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") during a [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). When [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is
"`navigate`", its [credentials mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is assumed to be
"`include`" and [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") does not currently account for other values. If
*HTML* changes here, this standard will need corresponding changes.
      
A [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") has an associated*use-URL-credentials 
flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.
   
When this flag is set, when a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s[URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") has a [username](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") and [password](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), and there is anavailable [authentication entry](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") for the [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), then the [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’scredentials are preferred over that of the [authentication entry](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). Modern specifications avoidsetting this flag, since putting credentials in [URLs](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is discouraged, but some olderfeatures set it for compatibility reasons.
   
A [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") has an associated*cache 
mode*, which is"`default`", "`no-store`", "`reload`","`no-cache`", "`force-cache`", or"`only-if-cached`". Unless stated otherwise, it is 
"`default`".
   

         "`default`"       [Fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") will inspect the HTTP cache on the way to the network. If the HTTP cache  contains a matching [fresh response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") it will be returned. If the HTTP cache contains a  matching [stale-while-revalidate response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") it will be returned, and a conditional network  fetch will be made to update the entry in the HTTP cache. If the HTTP cache contains a matching  [stale response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"), a conditional network fetch will be returned to update the entry in  the HTTP cache. Otherwise, a non-conditional network fetch will be returned to update the entry  in the HTTP cache. [[HTTP]](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") [[STALE-WHILE-REVALIDATE]](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")      
"`no-store`"       Fetch behaves as if there is no HTTP cache at all.       "`reload`"       Fetch 
behaves as if there is no HTTP cache on the way to the network. Ergo, it creates 
a  normal request and updates the HTTP cache with the response.       "`no-cache`"       
Fetch creates a conditional request if there is a response in the HTTP cache and 
a normal  request otherwise. It then updates the HTTP cache with the response.   
    "`force-cache`"       Fetch uses any response in the HTTP cache matching the request, not 
paying attention to  staleness. If there was no response, it creates a normal request 
and updates the HTTP cache with  the response.       "`only-if-cached`"       Fetch uses any response 
in the HTTP cache matching the request, not paying attention to  staleness. If there 
was no response, it returns a network error. (Can only be used when  [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s [mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is 
"`same-origin`". Any cached redirects will be followed assuming  [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s  [redirect mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is
"`follow`" and the  redirects do not violate [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s  [mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").)      
   
If [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [contains](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``If-Modified-Since`\`, 
\``If-None-Match`\`, \``If-Unmodified-Since`\`, \``If-Match`\`, or \``If-Range`\`, [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") will set [cache mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to
"`no-store`" if it is "`default`".
      
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*redirect mode*, which 
is"`follow`", "`error`", or "`manual`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`follow`".
   

         "`follow`"       Follow all redirects incurred when fetching a resource.      
 "`error`"       Return a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") when a request is met with a redirect.      
"`manual`"       Retrieves an [opaque-redirect filtered response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") when a request is met with a redirect,  to allow a service worker to replay the redirect offline. The response is otherwise  indistinguishable from a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), to not violate  [atomic HTTP redirect handling](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").      
     
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*integrity metadata*(a 
string). Unless stated otherwise, it is the empty string.
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*cryptographic nonce metadata*(a 
string). Unless stated otherwise, it is the empty string.
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*parser metadata*which 
is the empty string, "`parser-inserted`", or"`not-parser-inserted`". Unless otherwise stated, it is the empty string.
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [cryptographic nonce metadata](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and[parser metadata](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are generally populated from attributes and flags on the HTMLelement responsible for creating a [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). They are used by various algorithms
in*Content Security Policy* to determine whether requests or responses are to be 
blocked ina given context. [[CSP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*reload-navigation flag*.Unless 
stated otherwise, it is unset.
   
This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*history-navigation 
flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.
   
This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated boolean *user-activation*.Unless 
stated otherwise, it is false.
   
This is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated *WebDriver navigation 
id*(null or a string). Unless stated otherwise, it is null.
   
This is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated boolean *render-blocking*.Unless 
stated otherwise, it is false.
   
This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s render-blocking mechanism. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated *WebTransport-hash 
list* (a[WebTransport-hash list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise it is « ».
   
A *WebTransport-hash list* is a [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of zero or more[WebTransport-hashes](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
A *WebTransport-hash* is a [tuple](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")consisting of an *algorithm* 
(a [string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")) and a*value* (a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")).
   
This list is for exclusive use by `[WebTransport(url, options)](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` whenoptions 
contains `[serverCertificateHashes](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.
   
---
   
A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*URL list* (a [list](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") of one ormore [URLs](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise, it is a list containing a copy of[request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key")’s [URL](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new/choose "You can also press the 'y' key").
   
A [request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues "You can also press the 'y' key") has an
associated*current URL*. It is a pointer to thelast [URL](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") in [request](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese")’s [URL list](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese").
   
A [request](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese") has an associated*redirect 
count*.Unless stated otherwise, it is zero.
   
A [request](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean") has an associated*response 
tainting*,which is "`basic`", "`cors`", or "`opaque`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`basic`".
   
A [request](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*prevent no-cache 
cache-control header modification flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.
   
A [request](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated *done flag*.Unless 
stated otherwise, it is unset.
   
A [request](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*timing 
allow failed flag*. Unless statedotherwise, it is unset.
   
A [request](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*navigation 
timing allow values list* (a [list](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") of[lists](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [strings](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».
   
Each [item](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") in the [navigation timing allow values list](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3")holds the
\``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` values of one redirect [response](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") in anavigation’s redirect chain.
   
A [request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [URL list](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [current URL](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3"),[redirect count](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [response tainting](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"),[done flag](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [timing allow failed flag](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), and[navigation timing allow values list](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3") are used as bookkeeping details by the[fetch](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") algorithm.
   
A [request](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*WebDriver 
id*which is the result of [generating a random UUID](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3"), set when the [request](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") iscreated. [[WEBCRYPTO]](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3")
   
The [WebDriver id](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") is used by WebDriver-BiDi. It remains constantacross redirects, authentication attempts, and CORS-preflight fetches of an initial request.When a request is [cloned](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), the created request gets a unique[WebDriver id](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). [[WEBDRIVER-BIDI]](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")
   
---
   
A *subresource request* is a [request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3")whose [destination](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`audio`", "`audioworklet`","`font`", "`image`", "`json`", "`manifest`","`paintworklet`", "`script`", "`style`", "`text`","`track`", "`video`", "`xslt`", 
or the empty string.
   
A *non-subresource request* is a [request](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")whose [destination](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`document`", "`embed`","`frame`", "`iframe`", "`object`", "`report`","`serviceworker`", "`sharedworker`", or "`worker`".
   
A *navigation request* is a [request](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose[destination](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")
is"`document`", "`embed`", "`frame`", "`iframe`",or "`object`".
   
See [handle fetch](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") for usage of these terms.[[SW]](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3")
   
---
   

    
To compute the *redirect-taint* of a[request](#fetch-api "Content Security Policy Level 3") request, 
perform the following steps. They return"`same-origin`", "`same-site`", or "`cross-site`".
   

     1.       [Assert](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3"): request’s [origin](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`client`". 
     
2.       Let lastURL be null. 
     
3.       Let taint be "`same-origin`". 
     
4.       [For each](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3") url of request’s [URL list](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3"):  
     

       1.         If lastURL is null, then set lastURL to url and   [continue](#background-reading "Content Security Policy Level 3").   
       
2.         If url’s [origin](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not [same site](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3") with   lastURL’s [origin](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3") and request’s [origin](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   not [same site](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") with lastURL’s [origin](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return   "`cross-site`".   
       
3.         If url’s [origin](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not [same origin](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") with   lastURL’s [origin](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") and request’s [origin](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   not [same origin](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") with lastURL’s [origin](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then set   taint to "`same-site`".   
       
4.         Set lastURL to url.  
      

     
-       Return taint.
    

      

    
*Serializing a request origin*, given a [request](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3")
request, is torun these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3"): request’s [origin](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`client`". 
     
2.       If request’s [redirect-taint](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`same-origin`", then return "`null`". 
     
3.       Return request’s [origin](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly"), [serialized](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content").
    

      

    
*Byte-serializing a request origin*, given a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css "subtitle")
request,is to return the result of [serializing a request origin](h-entry "subtitle") with request,[isomorphic encoded](h-entry "subtitle").
      
---
   

    
To *clone* a[request](h-entry "subtitle") request, run these steps:
   

     1.       Let newRequest be a copy of request, except for its [body](h-entry "subtitle") and [WebDriver id](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Set newRequest’s [WebDriver id](h-entry "subtitle") to the result of [generating a random UUID](h-entry "subtitle"). [[WEBCRYPTO]](h-entry "subtitle") 
     
3.       If request’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") is non-null, set newRequest’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") to the result of [cloning](h-entry "subtitle") request’s [body](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Return newRequest.
    

      
---
   

    
To *add a range header* to a[request](h-entry "subtitle") request, with an integer 
first, and an optional integerlast, run these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): last is not given, or first is less than or equal to last. 
     
2.       Let rangeValue be \``bytes=`\`. 
     
3.       [Serialize](h-entry "subtitle") and [isomorphic encode](h-entry "subtitle") first, and append the result to rangeValue. 
     
4.       Append 0x2D (-) to rangeValue. 
     
5.       If last is given, then [serialize](h-entry "subtitle") and [isomorphic encode](h-entry "subtitle") it, and append the result to rangeValue. 
     
6.       [Append](h-entry "subtitle") (\``Range`\`, rangeValue) to request’s [header list](h-entry "subtitle").
    

    
A range header denotes an inclusive byte range. There a range header wherefirst is 
0 and last is 500, is a range of 501 bytes.
    
Features that combine multiple responses into one logical resource are historically 
asource of security bugs. Please seek security review for features that deal with 
partial responses.
      
---
   

    
To *serialize a response URL for reporting*, given a [response](h-entry "subtitle")response, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): response’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle") [is not empty](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let url be a copy of response’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle")[0].  
      
	This is not response’s [URL](h-entry "subtitle") in order to avoid  leaking information about redirect targets (see  [similar considerations for CSP reporting](h-entry "subtitle")  too). [[CSP]](h-entry "subtitle") 
     
3.       [Set the username](h-entry "subtitle") given url and the empty string. 
     
4.       [Set the password](h-entry "subtitle") given url and the empty string. 
     
5.       Return the [serialization](h-entry "subtitle") of url with [*exclude fragment*](h-entry "subtitle") set to true.
    

      

    
To check if *Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy allows credentials*, given a[request](h-entry "subtitle")
request, run these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): request’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is not "`client`". 
     
2.       If request’s [mode](h-entry "subtitle") is not "`no-cors`", then return true. 
     
3.       If request’s [client](h-entry "subtitle") is null, then return true. 
     
4.       If request’s [client](h-entry "subtitle")’s [policy container](h-entry "subtitle")’s [embedder policy](h-entry "subtitle")’s [value](h-entry "subtitle") is not "[`credentialless`](h-entry "subtitle")", then return true. 
     
5.       If request’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is [same origin](h-entry "subtitle") with request’s [current URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") and request’s [redirect-taint](h-entry "subtitle") is not "`same-origin`", then return true. 
     
6.       Return false.
    

      
#### 2.2.6. Responses
   
The result of [fetch](h-entry "subtitle") is a*response*. A [response](h-entry "subtitle")evolves over time. That is, not all its fields are available straight away.
   
A [response](h-entry "subtitle") has an associated*type* which is"`basic`","`cors`","`default`","`error`","`opaque`", 
or"`opaqueredirect`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`default`".
   
A [response](h-entry "subtitle") can have an associated*aborted flag*, which is initially 
unset.
   
This indicates that the request was intentionally aborted by the developer orend-user.
   
A [response](h-entry "subtitle") has an associated*URL*. It is a pointer to the last[URL](h-entry "subtitle") in [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle") and null if[response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle") [is empty](h-entry "subtitle").
   
A [response](h-entry "subtitle") has an associated*URL list* (a [list](h-entry "subtitle") of zero ormore [URLs](h-entry "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».
   
Except for the first and last [URL](h-entry "subtitle"), if any, a [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s[URL list](h-entry "subtitle") is not directly exposed to script as that would violate[atomic HTTP redirect handling](h-entry "subtitle").
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*status*, 
which is a [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").Unless stated otherwise it is 200.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*status 
message*. Unless statedotherwise it is the empty byte sequence.
   
Responses over an HTTP/2 connection will always have the empty byte sequence as statusmessage 
as HTTP/2 does not support them.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*header 
list* (a[header list](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise it is « ».
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*body* 
(null or a[body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise it is null.
   
The [source](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") and [length](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") concepts of a network’s[response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") are always null.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*cache 
state* (the empty string,"`local`", or "`validated`"). Unless stated otherwise, it is the emptystring.
   
This is intended for usage by *Service Workers* and*Resource Timing*.  [[SW]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*CORS-exposed 
header-name list*(a list of zero or more [header](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")[names](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")). The list is empty unless otherwise specified.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") will typically get its[CORS-exposed header-name list](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") set by [extracting header values](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") from the\`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")\` header. This list is used by a[CORS filtered response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to determine which headers to expose.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated*range-requested 
flag*, which isinitially unset.
   
This is used to prevent a partial response from an earlier ranged request beingprovided 
to an API that didn’t make a range request. See the flag’s usage for a detailed 
descriptionof the attack.
   
A [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has an associated
*request-includes-credentials*(a boolean), which is initially true.
   
A [response](# "subtitle") has an associated*timing allow passed flag*,which is initially 
unset.
   
This is used so that the caller to a fetch can determine if sensitive timing data 
isallowed on the resource fetched by looking at the flag of the response returned. 
Because the flag onthe response of a redirect has to be set if it was set for previous 
responses in the redirect chain,this is also tracked internally using the request’s 
[timing allow failed flag](# "subtitle").
   
A [response](# "subtitle") has an associated*navigation timing allow values list* 
(a [list](# "subtitle") of[lists](# "subtitle") of [strings](# "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».
   
This holds the \``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` values of each redirect[response](# "subtitle") in a navigation’s redirect chain. It is used so that callers can determine,once the navigation’s destination origin is known, whether that origin is allowed by every redirectin the redirect chain.
   
A [response](# "subtitle") has an associated*body info*(a [response body info](# "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise, it is a new[response body info](# "subtitle").
   
A [response](# "subtitle") has an associated*service worker timing info* (null or 
a[service worker timing info](# "subtitle")), which is initially null.
   
A [response](# "subtitle") has an associated *redirect taint*("`same-origin`", "`same-site`", or "`cross-site`"), 
which isinitially "`same-origin`".
   
---
   
A *network error* is a [response](# "subtitle") whose[type](# "subtitle") is "`error`", 
[status](# "subtitle") is 0,[status message](# "subtitle") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](# "subtitle") is « », [body](# "subtitle") is null, and[body info](# "subtitle") is a new [response body info](# "subtitle").
   
An *aborted network error* is a[network error](# "subtitle") whose [aborted flag](# "subtitle") is set.
   

    
To create the *appropriate network error* given [fetch params](# "subtitle")fetchParams:
   

     1.       [Assert](# "subtitle"): fetchParams is [canceled](# "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Return an [aborted network error](# "subtitle") if fetchParams is [aborted](# "subtitle"); otherwise return a [network error](# "subtitle").
    

      
---
   
A *filtered response* is a [response](# "subtitle")that offers a limited view on an associated [response](# "subtitle"). This associated[response](# "subtitle") can be accessed through [filtered response](#
"subtitle")’s*internal response* (a[response](# "subtitle") that is neither a [network error](# "subtitle") nor a[filtered response](# "subtitle")).
   
Unless stated otherwise a [filtered response](# "subtitle")’s associated concepts (such as its[body](# "subtitle")) refer to the associated concepts of its[internal response](# "subtitle"). (The exceptions to this are listed below as partof defining the concrete types of [filtered responses](# "subtitle").)
   

    
The [fetch](# "subtitle") algorithm by way of [*processResponse*](# "subtitle") and equivalent parameters exposes [filtered responses](# "subtitle") to callers to ensure they do not accidentally leak information. If the information needs to be revealed for legacy reasons, e.g., to feed image data to a decoder, the associated [internal response](# "subtitle") can be used by specification algorithms. 
    
New specifications ought not to build further on [opaque filtered responses](# "subtitle") or [opaque-redirect filtered responses](# "subtitle"). Those are legacy constructs and cannot always be adequately protected given contemporary computer architecture.
      
A *basic filtered response* is a[filtered response](# "subtitle") whose[type](# "subtitle") is "`basic`" 
and[header list](# "subtitle") excludes any[headers](# "subtitle") in[internal response](# "subtitle")’s[header list](# "subtitle") whose[name](# "subtitle") is a[forbidden response-header name](# "subtitle").
   
A *CORS filtered response* is a[filtered response](# "subtitle") whose[type](# "subtitle") is "`cors`" 
and[header list](# "subtitle") excludes any[headers](# "subtitle") in[internal response](# "subtitle")’s[header list](# "subtitle") whose[name](# "subtitle") is
*not* a[CORS-safelisted response-header name](# "subtitle"), given[internal response](# "subtitle")’s[CORS-exposed header-name list](# "subtitle").
   
An *opaque filtered response* is a[filtered response](# "subtitle") whose[type](# "subtitle") is
"`opaque`",[URL list](# "subtitle") is « »,[status](# "subtitle") is 0,[status message](# "subtitle") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](# "subtitle") is « »,[body](# "subtitle") is null, and[body info](# "subtitle") is a new [response body info](# "subtitle").
   
An*opaque-redirect filtered response*is a [filtered response](# "subtitle") whose[type](# "subtitle") is
"`opaqueredirect`",[status](# "subtitle") is 0,[status message](# "subtitle") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](# "subtitle") is « »,[body](# "subtitle") is null, and[body info](# "subtitle") is a new [response body info](# "subtitle").
   

    
Exposing the [URL list](# "subtitle") for [opaque-redirect filtered responses](# "subtitle") is harmless since no redirects are followed. 
    
In other words, an [opaque filtered response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") and an [opaque-redirect filtered response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") are nearly indistinguishable from a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). When introducing new APIs, do not use the [internal response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") for internal specification algorithms as that will leak information. 
    
This also means that JavaScript APIs, such as [`response.ok`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), will return rather useless results.
      

         
The [type](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") of a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is exposed to script through the `[type](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")` 
getter: 

```h-entry status-LS
console.log(new Response().type); // "default"

console.log((await fetch("/")).type); // "basic"

console.log((await fetch("https://api.example/status")).type); // "cors"

console.log((await fetch("https://crossorigin.example/image", { mode: "no-cors" })).type); // "opaque"

console.log((await fetch("/surprise-me", { redirect: "manual" })).type); // "opaqueredirect"
```
    
(This assumes that the various resources exist, `https://api.example/status` has the appropriate CORS headers, 
and `/surprise-me` uses a [redirect status](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").)
      
---
   

    
To *clone* a[response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") response, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       If response is a [filtered response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), then return a new identical [filtered response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") whose [internal response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is a [clone](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") of response’s [internal response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let newResponse be a copy of response, except for its [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"). 
     
3.       If response’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is non-null, then set newResponse’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") to the result of [cloning](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") response’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Return newResponse.
    

      
---
   
A *fresh response* is a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") whose[current age](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is within its [freshness lifetime](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").
   
A *stale-while-revalidate response* is a[response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") that is not a [fresh response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") and whose [current age](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is within the[stale-while-revalidate lifetime](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"). [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") [[STALE-WHILE-REVALIDATE]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
A *stale response* is a [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that isnot a [fresh response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") or a [stale-while-revalidate response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
---
   

    
The *location URL* of a[response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response, given 
null or an [ASCII string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")requestFragment, is the 
value returned by the following steps. They return null, failure,or a [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   

     1.       If response’s [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [redirect status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return null. 
     
2.       Let location be the result of [extracting header list values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given \``Location`\` and response’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
3.       If location is a [header value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set location to the  result of [parsing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") location with response’s  [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  
      
	If response was constructed through the `[Response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` constructor,  response’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") will be null, meaning that location will  only parse successfully if it is an [absolute-URL-with-fragment string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
4.       If location is a [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose [fragment](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is null, then set  location’s [fragment](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to requestFragment.  
      
	This ensures that synthetic (indeed, all) responses follow the processing model for  redirects defined by HTTP. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") 
     
5.       Return location.
    

    
The [location URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") algorithm is exclusively used for redirecthandling in this standard and in
*HTML*’s navigate algorithm which handles redirectsmanually. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
      
#### 2.2.7. Miscellaneous
   
A *potential destination* is"`fetch`" or a [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") which is not the empty string.
   

    
To *translate* a[potential destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") potentialDestination, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       If potentialDestination is "`fetch`", then return the empty string. 
     
2.       [Assert](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): potentialDestination is a [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Return potentialDestination.
    

      
### 2.3. Authentication entries
   
An *authentication entry* and a *proxy-authentication entry* aretuples of username, 
password, and realm, used for HTTP authentication and HTTP proxy authentication,and 
associated with one or more [requests](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
User agents should allow both to be cleared together with HTTP cookies and similar 
trackingfunctionality.
   
Further details are defined by HTTP. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   
### 2.4. Fetch groups
   
Each [environment settings object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*fetch 
group*, which holds a [fetch group](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
A *fetch group* holds information about fetches.
   
A [fetch group](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated:
       *fetch records*     A [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of [fetch records](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    
*deferred fetch records*     A [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of [deferred fetch records](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    
  
A *fetch record* is a [struct](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with the following[items](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"):
       *request*     A [request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle").     *controller* 
    A [fetch controller](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") or null.    
  
---
   
A *deferred fetch record* is a [struct](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key") used to maintain state needed toinvoke a fetch at a later time, e.g., when a document is unloaded or becomes not[fully active](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key"). It has the following [items](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese"):
       *request*     A [request](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese").     *notify 
invoked*     An algorithm accepting no arguments.     *invoke state* (default "`pending`") 
    "`pending`", "`sent`", or "`aborted`".      
---
   
When a [fetch group](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") fetchGroup is*terminated*:
  

    1.      [For each](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") [fetch record](#methods "Content Security Policy Level 3") record of fetchGroup’s [fetch records](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3"), if record’s [controller](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is non-null and record’s [request](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [done flag](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is unset and [keepalive](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3") is false, [terminate](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") record’s [controller](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
    
2.      [Process deferred fetches](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") for fetchGroup.
   

   
### 2.5. Resolving domains
   

    
[![(This is a tracking vector.)](idl "Content Security Policy Level 3")](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3")
 To*resolve an origin*, given a[network partition key](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3")
key and an [origin](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") origin:
   

     1.       If origin’s [host](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [IP address](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return « origin’s [host](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") ». 
     
2.       If origin’s [host](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [public suffix](#http-requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`localhost`" or "`localhost.`", then return « `::1`, `127.0.0.1` ». 
     
3.       Perform an [implementation-defined](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") operation to turn origin into a  [set](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") of one or more [IP addresses](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3").  
      
	It is also [implementation-defined](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") whether other operations might be performed to get  connection information beyond just [IP addresses](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). For example, if origin’s  [scheme](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [HTTP(S) scheme](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3"), the implementation might perform a DNS query  for HTTPS RRs. [[SVCB]](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")  
      
	If this operation succeeds, return the [set](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [IP addresses](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") and any  additional [implementation-defined](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") information. 
     
4.       Return failure.
    

    
The results of [resolve an origin](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") may be cached. If they are cached,
key shouldbe used as part of the cache key.
    

     
Typically this operation would involve DNS and as such caching can happen on DNS 
servers without key being taken into account. Depending on the implementation it 
might also not be possible to take key into account locally. [[RFC1035]](#http-network-or-cache-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") 
     
The order of the [IP addresses](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") that the [resolve an origin](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") algorithm can return can differ between invocations. 
     
The particulars (apart from the cache key) are not tied down as they are not pertinent 
to the system the Fetch Standard establishes. Other documents ought not to build 
on this primitive without having a considered discussion with the Fetch Standard 
community first.
          
### 2.6. Connections
   
A user agent has an associated *connection pool*. A[connection pool](#headers-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [ordered set](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3") of zero or
more*connections*. Each [connection](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") isidentified by an associated
*key* (a [network partition key](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3")),*origin* 
(an [origin](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")), and *credentials*(a 
boolean).
   
Each [connection](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an
associated*timing info* (a[connection timing info](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3")).
   
A *connection timing info* is a [struct](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") used to maintain timinginformation pertaining to the process of obtaining a connection. It has the following[items](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3"):
       *domain lookup start time* (default 0)     *domain lookup end time* (default 
0)     *connection start time* (default 0)     *connection end time* (default 0) 
    *secure connection start time* (default 0)     A `[DOMHighResTimeStamp](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level
3")`.     *ALPN negotiated protocol* (default the empty [byte sequence](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly"))    
A [byte sequence](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content").      

    
To *clamp and coarsen connection timing info*, given a[connection timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css "subtitle")
timingInfo, a `[DOMHighResTimeStamp](h-entry "subtitle")`defaultStartTime, and a boolean
crossOriginIsolatedCapability, run thesesteps:
   

     1.       If timingInfo’s [connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") is less than defaultStartTime, then return a new [connection timing info](h-entry "subtitle") whose [domain lookup start time](h-entry "subtitle") is defaultStartTime, [domain lookup end time](h-entry "subtitle") is defaultStartTime, [connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") is defaultStartTime, [connection end time](h-entry "subtitle") is defaultStartTime, [secure connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") is defaultStartTime, and [ALPN negotiated protocol](h-entry "subtitle") is timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Return a new [connection timing info](h-entry "subtitle") whose [domain lookup start time](h-entry "subtitle") is the result of [coarsen time](h-entry "subtitle") given timingInfo’s [domain lookup start time](h-entry "subtitle") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [domain lookup end time](h-entry "subtitle") is the result of [coarsen time](h-entry "subtitle") given timingInfo’s [domain lookup end time](h-entry "subtitle") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") is the result of [coarsen time](h-entry "subtitle") given timingInfo’s [connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [connection end time](h-entry "subtitle") is the result of [coarsen time](h-entry "subtitle") given timingInfo’s [connection end time](h-entry "subtitle") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [secure connection start time](h-entry "subtitle") is the result of [coarsen time](h-entry "subtitle") given timingInfo’s [connection end time](h-entry "subtitle") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, and [ALPN negotiated protocol](h-entry "subtitle") is timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](h-entry "subtitle").
    

      
---
   
A *new connection setting* is "`no`", "`yes`", or"`yes-and-dedicated`".
   

    
To *obtain a connection*, given a[network partition key](h-entry "subtitle") key, 
[URL](h-entry "subtitle") url, booleancredentials, an optional [new connection setting](h-entry "subtitle")
new (default"`no`"), an optional boolean*requireUnreliable* (default false), and anoptional 
[WebTransport-hash list](h-entry "subtitle")*webTransportHashes* (default « »):
   

     1.       If new is "`no`":  
     

       1.         [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): webTransportHashes [is empty](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
2.         Let connections be a set of [connections](h-entry "subtitle") in the user agent’s   [connection pool](h-entry "subtitle") whose [key](h-entry "subtitle") is key,   [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is url’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle"), and   [credentials](h-entry "subtitle") is credentials.   
       
3.         If connections is not empty and requireUnreliable is false, then   return one of connections.   
       
4.         If there is a [connection](h-entry "subtitle") capable of supporting unreliable transport in   connections, e.g., HTTP/3, then return that [connection](h-entry "subtitle").  
      

     
-       Let proxies be the result of finding proxies for url in an  [implementation-defined](h-entry "subtitle") manner. If there are no proxies, let proxies be  « "`DIRECT`" ».  
      
	This is where non-standard technology such as  [Web Proxy Auto-Discovery Protocol (WPAD)](h-entry "subtitle")  and [proxy auto-config (PAC)](h-entry "subtitle") come  into play. The "`DIRECT`" value means to not use a proxy for this particular  url. 
     
-       Let timingInfo be a new [connection timing info](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       [For each](h-entry "subtitle") proxy of proxies:  
     

       1.         Set timingInfo’s [domain lookup start time](h-entry "subtitle")   to the [unsafe shared current time](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
2.         Let hosts be « url’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle")’s   [host](h-entry "subtitle") ».   
       
3.         If proxy is "`DIRECT`", then set hosts to the result of   running [resolve an origin](h-entry "subtitle") given key and url’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
4.         If hosts is failure, then [continue](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
5.         Set timingInfo’s [domain lookup end time](h-entry "subtitle") to   the [unsafe shared current time](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
6.         Let connection be the result of running this step: run [create a connection](h-entry "subtitle")    given key, url’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle"), credentials,    proxy, an [implementation-defined](h-entry "subtitle") [host](h-entry "subtitle") from hosts,    timingInfo, requireUnreliable, and webTransportHashes an    [implementation-defined](h-entry "subtitle") number of times, [in parallel](h-entry "subtitle") from each other, and wait for    at least 1 to return a value. In an [implementation-defined](h-entry "subtitle") manner, select a value to    return from the returned values and return it. Any other returned values that are    [connections](h-entry "subtitle") may be closed.    
        
	Essentially this allows an implementation to pick one or more    [IP addresses](h-entry "subtitle") from the return value of [resolve an origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") (assuming    proxy is "`DIRECT`") and race them against each other, favor    [IPv6 addresses](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), retry in case of a timeout, etc.   
       
7.         If connection is failure, then [continue](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").   
       
8.         If new is not "`yes-and-dedicated`", then [append](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")   connection to the user agent’s [connection pool](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").   
       
9.         Return connection.  
      

     
-       Return failure.
    

    
This is intentionally a little vague as there are a lot of nuances to connectionmanagement 
that are best left to the discretion of implementers. Describing this helps explain 
the`<link rel=preconnect>` feature and clearly stipulates that [connections](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") arekeyed on [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). The latter clarifies that, e.g., TLS session identifiers are notreused across [connections](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") whose [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") are false with[connections](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") whose [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") are true.
      
---
   

    
To *create a connection*, given a [network partition key](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")
key,[origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") origin, boolean 
credentials, string proxy,[host](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") host, 
[connection timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") timingInfo,boolean 
requireUnreliable, and a [WebTransport-hash list](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css
"subtitle")webTransportHashes:
   

     1.       Set timingInfo’s [connection start time](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to the [unsafe shared current time](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let connection be a new [connection](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") whose [key](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is  key, [origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is origin,  [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is credentials, and [timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")  is timingInfo. [Record connection timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given connection  and use connection to establish an HTTP connection to host, taking  proxy and origin into account, with the following caveats: [[HTTP]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")  [[HTTP1]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") [[TLS]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")  
             3.         If requireUnreliable is true, then establish a connection capable of unreliable   transport, e.g., an HTTP/3 connection. [[HTTP3]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")   
       
4.         When establishing a connection capable of unreliable transport, enable options that are   necessary for WebTransport. For HTTP/3, this means including   `SETTINGS_ENABLE_WEBTRANSPORT` with a value of `1` and   `H3_DATAGRAM` with a value of `1` in the initial `SETTINGS`   frame. [[WEBTRANSPORT-HTTP3]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") [[HTTP3-DATAGRAM]](# "subtitle")   
       
5.         If credentials is false, then do not send a TLS client certificate.   
       
6.         If webTransportHashes [is not empty](# "subtitle"), instead of using the default   certificate verification algorithm, consider the server certificate valid if it meets the   [custom certificate requirements](# "subtitle") and if   [verifying the certificate hash](# "subtitle") against webTransportHashes returns   true. If either condition is not met, then return failure.   
       
7.         If establishing a connection does not succeed (e.g., a UDP, TCP, or TLS error), then   return failure.  
      
     
8.       
Set timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](# "subtitle") to  connection’s ALPN 
Protocol ID, with the following caveats: [[RFC7301]](# "subtitle")  
      
       9.         When a proxy is configured, if a tunnel connection is established then this must be the   ALPN Protocol ID of the tunneled protocol, otherwise it must be the ALPN Protocol ID of the first   hop to the proxy.   
       
10.         In case the user agent is using an experimental, non-registered protocol, the user agent must    use the used ALPN Protocol ID, if any. If ALPN was not used for protocol negotiations, the user    agent may use another descriptive string.    
        
	timingInfo’s    [ALPN negotiated protocol](# "subtitle") is intended to identify the network    protocol in use regardless of how it was actually negotiated; that is, even if ALPN is not used    to negotiate the network protocol, this is the ALPN Protocol IDs that indicates the protocol in    use.  
      
      
IANA maintains a  [list of ALPN Protocol IDs](# "subtitle"). 
     
11.       
Return connection.
    

      
---
   

    
To *record connection timing info* given a [connection](# "subtitle")connection, 
let timingInfo be connection’s[timing info](# "subtitle") and observe these requirements:
    
     -       timingInfo’s [connection end time](# "subtitle") should be the  [unsafe shared current time](# "subtitle") immediately after establishing the connection to the  server or proxy, as follows:  
             -         The returned time must include the time interval to establish the transport connection, as   well as other time intervals such as SOCKS authentication. It must include the time interval to   complete enough of the TLS handshake to request the resource.   
       
-         If the user agent used TLS False Start for this connection, this interval must not include   the time needed to receive the server’s Finished message. [[RFC7918]](# "subtitle")   
       
-         If the user agent sends the request with early data without waiting for the full handshake   to complete, this interval must not include the time needed to receive the server’s ServerHello   message. [[RFC8470]](# "subtitle")   
       
-         If the user agent waits for full handshake completion to send the request, this interval   includes the full TLS handshake even if other requests were sent using early data on   connection.  
      
      
Suppose the user agent establishes an HTTP/2  connection over TLS 1.3 to send a `GET` 
request and a `POST` request. It  sends the ClientHello at time t1 and then sends the 
`GET` request with early  data. The `POST` request is not safe ([[HTTP]](# "subtitle"), section 9.2.1), so the user  agent waits to complete the handshake at time
t2 before sending it. Although both  requests used the same connection, the `GET` request 
reports a connection end time of  t1, while the `POST` request reports t2. 
     
-       
If a secure transport is used, timingInfo’s [secure connection start time](# "subtitle") should be the result of calling [unsafe shared current time](# "subtitle") immediately before starting the handshake process to secure
connection. [[TLS]](# "subtitle") 
     
-       
If connection is an HTTP/3 connection, timingInfo’s [connection start time](# "subtitle") and
timingInfo’s [secure connection start time](# "subtitle") must be equal. (In HTTP/3 the secure transport handshake process is performed as part of the initial connection setup.) [[HTTP3]](# "subtitle")
    
    
The [clamp and coarsen connection timing info](# "subtitle") algorithm ensures thatdetails of reused connections are not exposed and time values are coarsened.
      
### 2.7. Network partition keys
   
A *network partition key* is a tuple consisting of a [site](# "subtitle") and nullor an [implementation-defined](# "subtitle") value.
   

    
To *determine the network partition key*, given an[environment](# "subtitle") environment:
   

     1.       Let topLevelOrigin be environment’s [top-level origin](# "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If topLevelOrigin is null, then set topLevelOrigin to environment’s [top-level creation URL](# "subtitle")’s [origin](# "subtitle"). 
     
3.       [Assert](# "subtitle"): topLevelOrigin is an [origin](# "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Let topLevelSite be the result of [obtaining a site](# "subtitle"), given topLevelOrigin. 
     
5.       Let secondKey be null or an [implementation-defined](# "subtitle") value.  
      
	The second key is intentionally a little vague as the finer points are still  evolving. See [issue #1035](# "subtitle"). 
     
6.       Return (topLevelSite, secondKey).
    

      

    
To *determine the network partition key*, given a [request](# "subtitle")request:
   

     1.       If request’s [reserved client](# "subtitle") is non-null, then return the result of [determining the network partition key](# "subtitle") given request’s [reserved client](# "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If request’s [client](# "subtitle") is non-null, then return the result of [determining the network partition key](# "subtitle") given request’s [client](# "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Return null.
    

      
### 2.8. HTTP cache partitions
   

    
To *determine the HTTP cache partition*, given a [request](# "subtitle") request:
   

     1.       Let key be the result of [determining the network partition key](# "subtitle") given request. 
     
2.       If key is null, then return null. 
     
3.       Return the unique HTTP cache associated with key. [[HTTP-CACHING]](# "subtitle")
    

      
### 2.9. Port blocking
   
New protocols can avoid the need for blocking ports by negotiating the protocolthrough 
TLS using ALPN. The protocol cannot be spoofed through HTTP requests in that case.[[RFC7301]](# "subtitle")
   

    
To determine whether fetching a [request](# "subtitle") request*should be blocked 
due to a bad port*:
   

     1.       Let url be request’s [current URL](# "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If url’s [scheme](# "subtitle") is an [HTTP(S) scheme](# "subtitle") and url’s [port](# "subtitle") is a [bad port](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), then return **blocked**. 
     
3.       Return **allowed**.
    

      
A [port](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is a*bad port* if it is 
listed in the first column of the following table.

### 2.10. Shouldresponse to request be blocked due to its MIME type?
    
Run these steps:
   

     1.       Let mimeType be the result of [extracting a MIME type](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") from response’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If mimeType is failure, then return **allowed**. 
     
3.       Let destination be request’s [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
4.       If destination is [script-like](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and one of the  following is true, then return **blocked**:  
             5. mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [starts with](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   "`audio/`", "`image/`", or "`video/`".          
6. mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`text/csv`".        
     
7.       
Return **allowed**.
    

      
## 3. HTTP extensions
   
### 3.1. Cookies
   
The \``Cookie`\` request header and \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers arelargely defined in their 
own specifications. We define additional infrastructure to be able to usethem conveniently 
here. [[COOKIES]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
#### 3.1.1. \``Cookie`\` header
   

    
To *append a request \``Cookie`\` header*, given a [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")request:
   

     1.       If the user agent is configured to disable cookies for request, then it should return. 
     
2.       Let sameSite be the result of [determining the same-site mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for request. 
     
3.       Let isSecure be true if request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`https`"; otherwise false. 
     
4.       Let httpOnlyAllowed be true.  
      
	True follows from this being invoked from [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), as opposed to the  `document.cookie` getter steps for instance. 
     
5.       Let cookies be the result of running [retrieve cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given isSecure,  request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), request’s  [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [path](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), httpOnlyAllowed, and sameSite.  
      
	The cookie store returns an ordered list of cookies 
     
6.       If cookies [is empty](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return. 
     
7.       Let value be the result of running [serialize cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given cookies. 
     
8.       [Append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (\``Cookie`\`, value) to request’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
    

      
#### 3.1.2. \``Set-Cookie`\` header
   

    
To *parse and store response \``Set-Cookie`\` headers*, given a[request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
request and a [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response:
   

     1.       If the user agent is configured to disable cookies for request, then it should return. 
     
2.       Let allowNonHostOnlyCookieForPublicSuffix be false. 
     
3.       Let isSecure be true if request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`https`"; otherwise false. 
     
4.       Let httpOnlyAllowed be true.  
      
	True follows from this being invoked from [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), as opposed to the  `document.cookie` getter steps for instance. 
     
5.       Let sameSiteStrictOrLaxAllowed be true if the result of [determine the same-site mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for request is "`strict-or-less`"; otherwise false. 
     
6.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") header of response’s  [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"):  
     

       1.         If header’s [name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") match   for \``Set-Cookie`\`, then [continue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   
       
2.         [Parse and store a cookie](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given header’s [value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),   isSecure, request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),   request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [path](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), httpOnlyAllowed,   allowNonHostOnlyCookieForPublicSuffix, and sameSiteStrictOrLaxAllowed.   
       
3.         [Garbage collect cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s   [host](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle").  
      

      As noted elsewhere the \``Set-Cookie`\` header cannot be combined and  therefore each occurrence is processed independently. This is not allowed for any other header.
    

      
#### 3.1.3. Cookie infrastructure
   

    
To *determine the same-site mode* for a given [request](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese")
request:
   

     1.       [Assert](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese"): request’s [method](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese") is "`GET`" or "`POST`". 
     
2.       If request’s [top-level navigation initiator origin](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese") is not null and is not [same site](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Content Security Policy Level 3") with request’s [URL](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [origin](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return "`unset-or-less`". 
     
3.       If request’s [method](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`GET`" and request’s [destination](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "document", then return "`lax-or-less`". 
     
4.       If request’s [client](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [has cross-site ancestor](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") is true, then return "`unset-or-less`". 
     
5.       If request’s [redirect-taint](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`cross-site`", then return "`unset-or-less`". 
     
6.       Return "`strict-or-less`".
    

      

    
To obtain a *serialized cookie default path* given a [URL](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level
3")url:
   

     1.       Let cloneURL be a clone of url. 
     
2.       Set cloneURL’s [path](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") to the [cookie default path](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") of cloneURL’s [path](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
     
3.       Return the [URL path serialization](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") of cloneURL.
    

      
### 3.2. \``Origin`\` header
   
The \`*`Origin`*\`request [header](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") indicates where a[fetch](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") originates from.
   
The \`[`Origin`](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` header is a version of
the\``Referer`\` [sic] header that does not reveal a [path](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). It is used for all[HTTP fetches](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose [request](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[response tainting](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`cors`", as well as those where[request](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [method](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") is neither
\``GET`\` nor\``HEAD`\`. Due to compatibility constraints it is not included in all[fetches](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3").
   
Its possible [values](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") are all the return values of[byte-serializing a request origin](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3"), given a [request](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). These are represented bythe following [ABNF](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"):

```secno
*serialized-ipv4*   = [dec-octet](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")
*dec-octet*         = DIGIT                 ; 0-9
/ %x31-39 DIGIT         ; 10-99
/ "1" 2DIGIT            ; 100-199
/ "2" %x30-34 DIGIT     ; 200-249
/ "25" %x30-35          ; 250-255

*serialized-ipv6*   =                            7( h16 ":" ) h16
/                       "::" 5( h16 ":" ) h16
/ [               h16 ] "::" 4( h16 ":" ) h16
/ [ *1( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::" 3( h16 ":" ) h16
/ [ *2( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::" 2( h16 ":" ) h16
/ [ *3( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"    h16 ":"   h16
/ [ *4( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"              h16
/ [ *5( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"
*h16*               = "0" / ( non-zero-hex 0*3hex )
*non-zero-hex*      = %x31-39 / %x61-66 ; '1'-'9' or lowercase 'a'-'f'
*hex*               = %x30-39 / %x61-66 ; '0'-'9' or lowercase 'a'-'f

*lower-alpha*       = %x61-7A
*lower-alphanum*    = [lower-alpha](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3") / DIGIT
*domain-label*      = [lower-alphanum](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") / ( [lower-alphanum](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") *( [lower-alphanum](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") / "-" ) [lower-alphanum](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") )
*serialized-domain* = *( [domain-label](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." ) [domain-label](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3")

*serialized-scheme* = [lower-alpha](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3") *( [lower-alphanum](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") / "+" / "-" / "." )
*serialized-host*   = [serialized-ipv4](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers") / "[" [serialized-ipv6](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly") "]" / [serialized-domain](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content")
*serialized-port*   = 1*5DIGIT

*serialized-origin* = [serialized-scheme](h-entry "subtitle") "://" [serialized-host](h-entry "subtitle") [ ":" [serialized-port](h-entry "subtitle") ]
*origin-or-null*    = [serialized-origin](h-entry "subtitle") / %s"null" ; case-sensitive

Origin = [origin-or-null](h-entry "subtitle")```


This supplants the definition in *The Web Origin Concept*. [[ORIGIN]](h-entry "subtitle")

The origin serialization defined here is more constrained than [[RFC3986]](h-entry "subtitle")’s grammar in twosubstantial ways. First, scheme and domains serializations are all lower case ASCII, withoutpercent encoding. Second, following the recommendations of
[*URL* § 3.6 Host serializing](h-entry "subtitle") and [[RFC5952]](h-entry "subtitle"),IPv6 addresses are limited as follows:

- The least-significant digits cannot be represented as an IPv4 address.       
- Leading zeros are forbidden.       
- All hex characters are lowercase.       
- "::" can’t elide only a single "0" block, so we allow at most 6 blocks when "::" is present.    

---


To *append a request \``Origin`\` header*,given a [request](h-entry "subtitle") request,
run these steps:


1.       [Assert](h-entry "subtitle"): request’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is not "`client`".

2.       Let serializedOrigin be the result of [byte-serializing a request origin](h-entry "subtitle") with request.

3.       If request’s [response tainting](h-entry "subtitle") is "`cors`" or request’s [mode](h-entry "subtitle") is either "`websocket`" or "`webtransport`", then [append](h-entry "subtitle") (\``Origin`\`, serializedOrigin) to request’s [header list](h-entry "subtitle").

4.       Otherwise, if request’s [method](h-entry "subtitle") is neither \``GET`\` nor  \``HEAD`\`, then:  


1.         If request’s [mode](h-entry "subtitle") is not "`cors`",    then switch on request’s [referrer policy](h-entry "subtitle"):    
"`no-referrer`"                        Set serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
"`no-referrer-when-downgrade`"              "`strict-origin`"              "`strict-origin-when-cross-origin`"                        If request’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is a [tuple origin](h-entry "subtitle"), its     scheme is "`https`", and request’s     [current URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s scheme is not "`https`", then set     serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
"`same-origin`"                        If request’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle") is not [same origin](h-entry "subtitle") with     request’s [current URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle"), then set     serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
Otherwise              Do nothing.                   
2.         [Append](h-entry "subtitle") (\``Origin`\`, serializedOrigin) to   request’s [header list](h-entry "subtitle").  


A [request](h-entry "subtitle")’s [referrer policy](h-entry "subtitle") is taken into account forall fetches where the fetcher did not explicitly opt into sharing their [origin](h-entry "subtitle") with theserver, e.g., via using the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle").

### 3.3. CORS protocol

To allow sharing responses cross-origin and allow for more versatile[fetches](h-entry "subtitle") than possible with HTML’s`[form](h-entry "subtitle")`
element, the *CORS protocol* exists. Itis layered on top of HTTP and allows responses
to declare they can be shared with other[origins](h-entry "subtitle").

It needs to be an opt-in mechanism to prevent leaking data from responses behind
afirewall (intranets). Additionally, for [requests](h-entry "subtitle") including[credentials](h-entry "subtitle") it needs to be opt-in to prevent leaking potentially-sensitive data.

This section explains the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") as it pertains to server developers.Requirements for user agents are part of the [fetch](h-entry "subtitle") algorithm,except for the [new HTTP header syntax](h-entry "subtitle").

#### 3.3.1. General

The [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") consists of a set of headers that indicates whether a response canbe shared cross-origin.

For [requests](h-entry "subtitle") that are more involved than what is possible with HTML’s `[form](h-entry
"subtitle")`element, a [CORS-preflight request](h-entry "subtitle") is performed, to ensure [request](h-entry "subtitle")’s[current URL](h-entry "subtitle") supports the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle").

#### 3.3.2. HTTP requests

A *CORS request* is an HTTP request that includes an\`[`Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` header. It cannot be reliably identified as participatingin the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") as the \`[`Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` header is also includedfor all [requests](h-entry "subtitle") whose [method](h-entry "subtitle") is neither
\``GET`\` nor\``HEAD`\`.

A *CORS-preflight request* is a [CORS request](h-entry "subtitle")that checks to see if the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") is understood. It uses
\``OPTIONS`\` as[method](h-entry "subtitle") and includes the following [header](h-entry "subtitle"):
\`*`Access-Control-Request-Method`*\`          
Indicates which [method](h-entry "subtitle") a future [CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") to the same resource might use.

A [CORS-preflight request](h-entry "subtitle") can also include the following [header](h-entry "subtitle"):
\`*`Access-Control-Request-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](h-entry "subtitle") a future [CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") to the same resource might use.

#### 3.3.3. HTTP responses

An HTTP response to a [CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") can include the following[headers](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"):
\`*`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`*\`          
Indicates whether the response can be shared, via returning the literal [value](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") of the \`[`Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")\` request [header](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") (which can be
\``null`\`) or \``*`\` in a response.
\`*`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`*\`
