Indicates whether the response can be shared when [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s  [credentials mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is
"`include`".  

For a [CORS-preflight request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s  [credentials mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is always
"`same-origin`", i.e., it excludes  credentials, but for any subsequent [CORS requests](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") it might not be. Support therefore needs  to be indicated as part of the HTTP response to the [CORS-preflight request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") as well.

An HTTP response to a [CORS-preflight request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") can include the following[headers](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"):
\`*`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`*\`          
Indicates which [methods](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") are supported by the [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s  [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").  

The \``Allow`\` [header](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is  not relevant for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").
\`*`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") are supported by the [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").
\`*`Access-Control-Max-Age`*\`          
Indicates the number of seconds (5 by default) the information provided by the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")\` and \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")\` [headers](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") can be cached.

An HTTP response to a [CORS request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") that is not a[CORS-preflight request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") can also include the following[header](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"):
\`*`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](# "subtitle") can be exposed as part of the response by listing their [names](# "subtitle").

---

A successful HTTP response, i.e., one where the server developer intends to share
it, to a[CORS request](# "subtitle") can use any [status](# "subtitle"), as long as it includes the [headers](# "subtitle")stated above with [values](# "subtitle") matching up with the request.

A successful HTTP response to a [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle") is similar, except it is restrictedto an [ok status](# "subtitle"), e.g., 200 or 204.

Any other kind of HTTP response is not successful and will either end up not being
shared or failthe [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle"). Be aware that any work the server performs might nonetheless leakthrough side channels, such as timing. If server developers wish to denote this explicitly, the 403[status](# "subtitle") can be used, coupled with omitting the relevant [headers](# "subtitle").

If desired, “failure” could also be shared, but that would make it a successful
HTTPresponse. That is why for a successful HTTP response to a [CORS request](# "subtitle") that is not a[CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle") the [status](# "subtitle") can be anything, including 403.

Ultimately server developers have a lot of freedom in how they handle HTTP responses
and thesetactics can differ between the response to the [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle") and the[CORS request](# "subtitle") that follows it:

-      They can provide a static response. This can be helpful when working with caching intermediaries. A static response can both be successful and not successful depending on the [CORS request](# "subtitle"). This is okay.

-      They can provide a dynamic response, tuned to [CORS request](# "subtitle"). This can be helpful when the response body is to be tailored to a specific origin or a response needs to have credentials and be successful for a set of origins.


#### 3.3.4. HTTP new-header syntax

[ABNF](# "subtitle") for the [values](# "subtitle") of the[headers](# "subtitle") used by the [CORS protocol](# "subtitle"):

```h-entry status-LS
Access-Control-Request-Method    = [method](# "subtitle")
Access-Control-Request-Headers   = 1#[field-name](# "subtitle")

*wildcard*                         = "*"
Access-Control-Allow-Origin      = [origin-or-null](# "subtitle") / [wildcard](# "subtitle")

Access-Control-Allow-Credentials = %s"true" ; case-sensitive
Access-Control-Expose-Headers    = #[field-name](# "subtitle")
Access-Control-Max-Age           = [delta-seconds](# "subtitle")
Access-Control-Allow-Methods     = #[method](# "subtitle")
Access-Control-Allow-Headers     = #[field-name](# "subtitle")```
   
For \``Access-Control-Expose-Headers`\`,\``Access-Control-Allow-Methods`\`, and \``Access-Control-Allow-Headers`\`response [headers](# "subtitle"), the [value](# "subtitle") \``*`\` 
counts as a wildcard for[requests](# "subtitle") without [credentials](# "subtitle"). For such [requests](# "subtitle") there is noway to solely match a [header name](# "subtitle") or [method](# "subtitle") that is
\``*`\`.
   
#### 3.3.5. CORS protocol and credentials
   
When [request](# "subtitle")’s[credentials mode](# "subtitle") is "`include`" ithas an 
impact on the functioning of the [CORS protocol](# "subtitle") other than including[credentials](# "subtitle") in the [fetch](# "subtitle").
   

         
In the old days, `[XMLHttpRequest](# "subtitle")` could be used to set [request](# "subtitle")’s [credentials mode](# "subtitle") to
"`include`": 

```h-entry status-LS
var client = new XMLHttpRequest()
client.open("GET", "./")
client.withCredentials = true
/* … */
```
    
Nowadays, `fetch("./", { credentials:"include" }).then(/* … */)` suffices.
      
A [request](# "subtitle")’s[credentials mode](# "subtitle") is not necessarily observableon the server; only when [credentials](# "subtitle") exist for a[request](# "subtitle") can it be observed by virtue of the[credentials](# "subtitle") being included. Note that even so, a [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle")never includes [credentials](# "subtitle").
   
The server developer therefore needs to decide whether or not responses "tainted" 
with[credentials](# "subtitle") can be shared. And also needs to decide if[requests](# "subtitle") necessitating a [CORS-preflight request](# "subtitle") caninclude [credentials](# "subtitle"). Generally speaking, both sharing responses and allowing requestswith [credentials](# "subtitle") is rather unsafe, and extreme care has to be taken to avoid the[confused deputy problem](# "subtitle").
   
To share responses with [credentials](# "subtitle"), the\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](# "subtitle")\` and\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](# "subtitle")\` [headers](# "subtitle") areimportant. The following table serves to illustrate the various legal and illegal combinations for arequest to
`https://rabbit.invalid/`:
   
|   |
||------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------|---------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| - | -                            | -                                                 | -                                                      | -       |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``*`\`                                           | Omitted                                                | ✅     | —​                                                                                                           |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``*`\`                                           | \``true`\`                                             | ✅     | If credentials mode is not "`include`", then  \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](# "subtitle")\` is ignored. |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``https://rabbit.invalid/`\`                     | Omitted                                                | ❌     | A [serialized](# "subtitle") origin has no trailing slash.                                                       |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                      | Omitted                                                | ✅     | —​                                                                                                           |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``*`\`                                           | \``true`\`                                             | ❌     | If credentials mode is "`include`", then  \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](# "subtitle")\` cannot be  \``*`\`.  |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                      | \``true`\`                                             | ✅     | —​                                                                                                           |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                      | \``True`\`                                             | ❌     | \``true`\` is (byte) case-sensitive.                                                                             |
   
Similarly, \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](# "subtitle")\`,\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`](# "subtitle")\`, and\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`](# "subtitle")\` response headers can only
use\``*`\` as value when [request](# "subtitle")’s [credentials mode](# "subtitle") is not"`include`".
   
#### 3.3.6. Examples
   

         
A script at `https://foo.invalid/` wants to fetch some data from `https://bar.invalid/`. (Neither [credentials](# "subtitle") nor response header access is important.) 

```h-entry status-LS
var url = "https://bar.invalid/api?key=730d67a37d7f3d802e96396d00280768773813fbe726d116944d814422fc1a45&data=about:unicorn";
fetch(url).then(success, failure)
```
    
This will use the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), though this is entirely transparent to the developer from
`foo.invalid`. As part of the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), the user agent will include the \`[`Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")\` header in the request: 

```h-entry status-LS
Origin: https://foo.invalid
```
    
Upon receiving a response from `bar.invalid`, the user agent will verify the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")\` response header. If its value is either
\``https://foo.invalid`\` or \``*`\`, the user agent will invoke the `success` callback. 
If it has any other value, or is missing, the user agent will invoke the `failure` callback.
      

         
The developer of `foo.invalid` is back, and now wants to fetch some data from `bar.invalid` while also 
accessing a response header. 

```h-entry status-LS
fetch(url).then(response => {
var hsts = response.headers.get("strict-transport-security"),
csp = response.headers.get("content-security-policy")
log(hsts, csp)
})
```
    
`bar.invalid` provides a correct \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")\` response header per the earlier example. The values of `hsts` 
and `csp` will depend on the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")\` response header. For example, if the response included the following headers 

```h-entry status-LS
Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'
Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubdomains; preload
Access-Control-Expose-Headers: Content-Security-Policy
```
    
then `hsts` would be null and `csp` would be "`default-src 'self'`", even though the response did include 
both headers. This is because `bar.invalid` needs to explicitly share each header by listing 
their names in the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")\` response header. 
    
Alternatively, if `bar.invalid` wanted to share all its response headers, for requests that 
do not include [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"), it could use
\``*`\` as value for the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")\` response header. If the request would have included [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"), the response header names would have to be listed explicitly and
\``*`\` could not be used.
      

         
The developer of `foo.invalid` returns, now fetching some data from `bar.invalid` while including [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"). This time around the [CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is no longer transparent to the developer as [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") require an explicit opt-in: 

```h-entry status-LS
fetch(url, { credentials:"include" }).then(success, failure)
```
    
This also makes any \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers `bar.invalid` includes fully functional (they are 
ignored otherwise). 
    
The user agent will make sure to include any relevant [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") in the request. It will also put stricter requirements on the response. Not only will `bar.invalid` 
need to list \``https://foo.invalid`\` as value for the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")\` header
(\``*`\` is not allowed when [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") are involved), the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")\` header has to be present too: 

```h-entry status-LS
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://foo.invalid
Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true
```
    
If the response does not include those two headers with those values, the `failure` callback 
will be invoked. However, any \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers will be respected.
      
#### 3.3.7. CORS protocol exceptions
   
Specifications have allowed limited exceptions to the CORS safelist for non-safelisted\``Content-Type`\` 
header values. These exceptions are made for requests that can betriggered by web 
content but whose headers and bodies can be only minimally controlled by the webcontent. 
Therefore, servers should expect cross-origin web content to be allowed to triggernon-preflighted 
requests with the following non-safelisted \``Content-Type`\` headervalues:
   
    - \``application/csp-report`\` [[CSP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")     
- \``application/expect-ct-report+json`\` [[RFC9163]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")     
- \``application/xss-auditor-report`\`     
- \``application/ocsp-request`\` [[RFC6960]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   
   
Specifications should avoid introducing new exceptions and should only do so with 
carefulconsideration for the security consequences. New exceptions can be proposed 
by[filing an issue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   
### 3.4. \``Content-Length`\` header
   
The \``Content-Length`\` header is largely defined in HTTP. Its processing model isdefined here 
as the model defined in HTTP is not compatible with web content. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   

    
To *extract a length*from a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") headers, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``Content-Length`\` from headers. 
     
2.       If values is null, then return null. 
     
3.       Let candidateValue be null. 
     
4.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") value of values:  
     

       1.         If candidateValue is null, then set candidateValue to   value.   
       
2.         Otherwise, if value is not candidateValue, return failure.  
      

     
-       If candidateValue is the empty string or has a [code point](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is not an [ASCII digit](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return null. 
     
-       Return candidateValue, interpreted as decimal number.
    

      
### 3.5. \``Content-Type`\` header
   
The \``Content-Type`\` header is largely defined in HTTP. Its processing model isdefined here 
as the model defined in HTTP is not compatible with web content. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
   

    
To*extract a MIME type*from a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") headers, 
run these steps. They return failure or a[MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
   

     1.       Let charset be null. 
     
2.       Let essence be null. 
     
3.       Let mimeType be null. 
     
4.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``Content-Type`\` from headers. 
     
5.       If values is null, then return failure. 
     
6.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") value of values:  
     

       1.         Let temporaryMimeType be the result of [parsing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   value.   
       
2.         If temporaryMimeType is failure or its [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is   "`*/*`", then [continue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   
       
3.         Set mimeType to temporaryMimeType.   
       
4.         If mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not essence, then:    
       

         1.           Set charset to null.     
         
2.           If mimeType’s [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"]     [exists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set charset to mimeType’s     [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"].     
         
3.           Set essence to mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    
        

       
-         Otherwise, if mimeType’s   [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"] does not [exist](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), and   charset is non-null, set mimeType’s   [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"] to charset.  
      

     
-       If mimeType is null, then return failure. 
     
-       Return mimeType.
    

      
When [extract a MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") returns failure or a [MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose[essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is incorrect for a given format, treat this as a fatal error.Existing web platform features have not always followed this pattern, which has been a major sourceof security vulnerabilities in those features over the years. In contrast, a[MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") can typically be safely ignored.
   

         
This is how [extract a MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") functions in practice: 
    
|                                                        |
||-----------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| -                                                      | -                           |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/plain;charset=gbk, text/html        |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html`                 |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk;a=b, text/html;x=y |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html;x=y;charset=gbk` |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk;a=b                |
| Content-Type: text/html;x=y                            |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk                    |
| Content-Type: x/x                                      |
| Content-Type: text/html;x=y                            |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html;x=y`             |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type: cannot-parse                             |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html`                 |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type: */*                                      |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type:                                          |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |
      

    
To *legacy extract an encoding* given failure or a [MIME type](#goals "Content Security Policy Level
3")mimeType and an [encoding](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") fallbackEncoding, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       If mimeType is failure, then return fallbackEncoding. 
     
2.       If mimeType["`charset`"] does not [exist](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return fallbackEncoding. 
     
3.       Let tentativeEncoding be the result of [getting an encoding](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") from mimeType["`charset`"]. 
     
4.       If tentativeEncoding is failure, then return fallbackEncoding. 
     
5.       Return tentativeEncoding.
    

    

     
This algorithm allows mimeType to be failure so it can be more easily combined with 
[extract a MIME type](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
     
It is denoted as legacy as modern formats are to exclusively use [UTF-8](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3").
          
### 3.6. \``X-Content-Type-Options`\` header
   
The\`*`X-Content-Type-Options`*\`response [header](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3") can be used to require checking of a [response](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level
3")’s\``Content-Type`\` [header](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") against the [destination](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") of a[request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3").
   

    
To *determine nosniff*, given a [header list](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3") list, 
runthese steps:
   

     1.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") \`[`X-Content-Type-Options`](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` from list. 
     
2.       If values is null, then return false. 
     
3.       If values[0] is an [ASCII case-insensitive](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for "`nosniff`", then return true. 
     
4.       Return false.
    

      
Web developers and conformance checkers must use the following [value](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3")[ABNF](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") for \`[`X-Content-Type-Options`](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3")\`:

```secno
X-Content-Type-Options           = "nosniff" ; case-insensitive
```
   

    
#### 3.6.1. Shouldresponse to request be blocked due to nosniff?
    
Run these steps:
   

     1.       If [determine nosniff](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") with response’s [header list](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is false, then return **allowed**. 
     
2.       Let mimeType be the result of [extracting a MIME type](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") from response’s [header list](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
     
3.       Let destination be request’s [destination](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
     
4.       If destination is [script-like](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") and mimeType is failure or is not a [JavaScript MIME type](#http-redirect-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return **blocked**. 
     
5.       If destination is "`style`" and mimeType is failure or its [essence](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`text/css`", then return **blocked**. 
     
6.       Return **allowed**.
    

    
Only [request](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3") [destinations](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") that are[script-like](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") or
"`style`" are considered as any exploitspertain to them. Also, considering "`image`" 
was not compatible with deployed content.
      
### 3.7. \``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`\` header
   
The\`*`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`*\`response [header](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3") can be used to require checking a [request](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[current URL](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [origin](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3") against a [request](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[origin](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3") when [request](#background-reading "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [mode](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")
is"`no-cors`".
   
Its [value](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3") [ABNF](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3"):

```secno
Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy     = %s"same-origin" / %s"same-site" / %s"cross-origin" ; case-sensitive
```
   

    
To perform a *cross-origin resource policy check*, given an [origin](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level
3")origin, an [environment settings object](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")
settingsObject, a stringdestination, a [response](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3")
response, and an optional booleanforNavigation, run these steps:
   

     1.       Set forNavigation to false if it is not given. 
     
2.       Let embedderPolicy be settingsObject’s [policy container](#index-defined-here "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [embedder policy](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3"). 
     
3.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") with origin,  "[`unsafe-none`](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3")", response, and  forNavigation returns **blocked**, then return **blocked**.  
      
	This step is needed because we don’t want to report violations not related to  Cross-Origin Embedder Policy below. 
     
4.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") with origin, embedderPolicy’s [report-only value](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers"), response, and forNavigation returns **blocked**, then [queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content") with response, settingsObject, destination, and true. 
     
5.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) with origin, embedderPolicy’s [value](h-entry "subtitle"), response, and forNavigation returns **allowed**, then return **allowed**. 
     
6.       [Queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report](h-entry "subtitle") with response, settingsObject, destination, and false. 
     
7.       Return **blocked**.
    

    
Only HTML’s navigate algorithm uses this check with forNavigation set totrue, and 
it’s always for nested navigations. Otherwise, response is either the[internal response](h-entry "subtitle") of an [opaque filtered response](h-entry "subtitle") or a[response](h-entry "subtitle") which will be the [internal response](h-entry "subtitle") of an[opaque filtered response](h-entry "subtitle"). [[HTML]](h-entry "subtitle")
      

    
To perform a *cross-origin resource policy internal check*, given an[origin](h-entry "subtitle")
origin, an [embedder policy value](h-entry "subtitle")embedderPolicyValue, a [response](h-entry "subtitle")
response, and a booleanforNavigation, run these steps:
   

     1.       If forNavigation is true and embedderPolicyValue is "[`unsafe-none`](h-entry "subtitle")", then return **allowed**. 
     
2.       Let policy be the result of [getting](h-entry "subtitle")  \`[`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`](h-entry "subtitle")\` from response’s  [header list](h-entry "subtitle").  
      
	This means that \``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site, same-origin`\`  ends up as **allowed** below as it will never match anything, as long as  embedderPolicyValue is "[`unsafe-none`](h-entry "subtitle")".  Two or more \`[`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`](h-entry "subtitle")\` headers will have the  same effect. 
     
3.       If policy is neither \``same-origin`\`, \``same-site`\`, nor \``cross-origin`\`, then set policy to null. 
     
4.       If policy is null, then switch on embedderPolicyValue:
             "[`unsafe-none`](h-entry "subtitle")"                  Do nothing.   
       "[`credentialless`](h-entry "subtitle")"                  Set policy to \``same-origin`\` if:    
                 5. response’s [request-includes-credentials](h-entry "subtitle") is true, or              
6. forNavigation is true.            
       "[`require-corp`](h-entry "subtitle")"                  
Set policy to \``same-origin`\`.  
           
7.       
Switch on policy:  
             null          \``cross-origin`\`                  
Return **allowed**.   
       \``same-origin`\`                  
If origin is [same origin](h-entry "subtitle") with response’s [URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s    [origin](h-entry "subtitle"), then return
**allowed**.    
        
Otherwise, return **blocked**.   
       \``same-site`\`                  
If all of the following are true    
        
         8.           origin is [schemelessly same site](h-entry "subtitle") with response’s     [URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s [origin](h-entry "subtitle")     
         
9.           origin’s [scheme](h-entry "subtitle") is "`https`" or     response’s [URL](h-entry "subtitle")’s [scheme](h-entry "subtitle") is not     "`https`"    
        
        
then return **allowed**.    
        
Otherwise, return **blocked**.    
        
\``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site`\` does not consider a    response delivered via a secure transport to match 
a non-secure requesting origin,    even if their hosts are otherwise same site. Securely-transported 
responses will only    match a securely-transported initiator.  
          

      

    
To *queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report*, given a[response](h-entry "subtitle")
response, an [environment settings object](h-entry "subtitle")settingsObject, a 
string destination, and a boolean reportOnly,run these steps:
   

     1.       Let endpoint be settingsObject’s [policy container](h-entry "subtitle")’s [embedder policy](h-entry "subtitle")’s [report-only reporting endpoint](h-entry "subtitle") if reportOnly is true and settingsObject’s [policy container](h-entry "subtitle")’s [embedder policy](h-entry "subtitle")’s [reporting endpoint](h-entry "subtitle") otherwise. 
     
2.       Let serializedURL be the result of [serializing a response URL for reporting](h-entry "subtitle") with response. 
     
3.       Let disposition be "`reporting`" if reportOnly is true; otherwise "`enforce`". 
     
4.       Let body be a new object containing the following properties:  
      
|   |
||-----------------|---------------|
| - | -               |
|   |
|   | "`type`"        | "`corp`"      |
|   | "`blockedURL`"  | serializedURL |
|   | "`destination`" | destination   |
|   | "`disposition`" | disposition   |
     
5.       [Generate and queue a report](h-entry "subtitle") for settingsObject’s [global object](h-entry "subtitle") given the ["`coep`" report type](h-entry "subtitle"), endpoint, and body. [[REPORTING]](h-entry "subtitle")
    

      
### 3.8. \``Sec-Purpose`\` header
   
The \`*`Sec-Purpose`*\` HTTP requestheader specifies that the request serves one or more purposes 
other than requesting the resource forimmediate use by the user.
   
The \`[`Sec-Purpose`](h-entry "subtitle")\` header field is a [structured header](h-entry "subtitle")whose value must be a [token](h-entry "subtitle").
   
The sole [token](h-entry "subtitle") defined is `prefetch`. Itindicates the request’s purpose 
is to fetch a resource that is anticipated to be needed shortly.
   
The server can use this to adjust the caching expiry for prefetches, to disallow 
theprefetch, or to treat it differently when counting page visits.
   
## 4. Fetching
   
The algorithm below defines [fetching](h-entry "subtitle"). In broad strokes, it takesa [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") and one or more algorithms to run at various points during the operation. A[response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is passed to the last two algorithms listed below. The first two algorithmscan be used to capture uploads.
   

    
To *fetch*, given a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") request, 
anoptional algorithm*processRequestBodyChunkLength*, anoptional
algorithm*processRequestEndOfBody*,an optional algorithm *processEarlyHintsResponse*, 
an optionalalgorithm *processResponse*, an optionalalgorithm *processResponseEndOfBody*, 
an optional algorithm*processResponseConsumeBody*,and an optional boolean *useParallelQueue* 
(default false), runthe steps below. If given, processRequestBodyChunkLength must 
be an algorithm acceptingan integer representing the number of bytes transmitted. 
If given,processRequestEndOfBody must be an algorithm accepting no arguments. If 
given,processEarlyHintsResponse must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). Ifgiven,
processResponse must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). If
given,processResponseEndOfBody must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). Ifgiven,
processResponseConsumeBody must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")and null, failure, or a [byte sequence](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").
    
The user agent may be asked to*suspend* the ongoing fetch.The user agent may either 
accept or ignore the suspension request. The suspended fetch can be*resumed*. The 
user agent shouldignore the suspension request if the ongoing fetch is updating the 
response in the HTTP cache forthe request.
    
The user agent does not update the entry in the HTTP cache for a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")if request’s cache mode is "no-store" or a \``Cache-Control:
no-store`\` header appears inthe response. [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")
   

     1.       [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"): request’s [mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is "`navigate`" or  processEarlyHintsResponse is null.  
      
	Processing of early hints ([responses](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") whose [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")  is 103) is only vetted for navigations. 
     
2.       Let taskDestination be null. 
     
3.       Let crossOriginIsolatedCapability be false. 
     
4.       [Populate request from client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given request. 
     
5.       If request’s [client](# "subtitle") is non-null, then:  
     

       1.         Set taskDestination to request’s [client](# "subtitle")’s   [global object](# "subtitle").   
       
2.         Set crossOriginIsolatedCapability to request’s   [client](# "subtitle")’s   [cross-origin isolated capability](# "subtitle").  
      

     
-       If useParallelQueue is true, then set taskDestination to the result of [starting a new parallel queue](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       Let timingInfo be a new [fetch timing info](# "subtitle") whose [start time](# "subtitle") and [post-redirect start time](# "subtitle") are the [coarsened shared current time](# "subtitle") given crossOriginIsolatedCapability, and [render-blocking](# "subtitle") is set to request’s [render-blocking](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       Let fetchParams be a new [fetch params](# "subtitle") whose [request](# "subtitle") is request, [timing info](# "subtitle") is timingInfo, [process request body chunk length](# "subtitle") is processRequestBodyChunkLength, [process request end-of-body](# "subtitle") is processRequestEndOfBody, [process early hints response](# "subtitle") is processEarlyHintsResponse, [process response](# "subtitle") is processResponse, [process response consume body](# "subtitle") is processResponseConsumeBody, [process response end-of-body](# "subtitle") is processResponseEndOfBody, [task destination](# "subtitle") is taskDestination, and [cross-origin isolated capability](# "subtitle") is crossOriginIsolatedCapability. 
     
-       If request’s [body](# "subtitle") is a [byte sequence](# "subtitle"), then set request’s [body](# "subtitle") to request’s [body](# "subtitle") [as a body](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       Run the [WebDriver BiDi clone network request body](# "subtitle") steps with request. 
     
-       If all of the following conditions are true:  
             -         request’s [URL](# "subtitle")’s [scheme](# "subtitle") is an   [HTTP(S) scheme](# "subtitle")   
       
-         request’s [mode](# "subtitle") is "`same-origin`",   "`cors`", or "`no-cors`"   
       
-         request’s [client](# "subtitle") is not null, and request’s   [client](# "subtitle")’s [global object](# "subtitle") is a   `[Window](# "subtitle")` object   
       
-         request’s [method](# "subtitle") is \``GET`\`   
       
-         request’s [unsafe-request flag](# "subtitle") is not set or   request’s [header list](# "subtitle") [is empty](# "subtitle")  
      
      
then:  
     

       1.         [Assert](# "subtitle"): request’s [origin](# "subtitle") is [same origin](# "subtitle")   with request’s [client](# "subtitle")’s   [origin](# "subtitle").   
       
2.         Let onPreloadedResponseAvailable be an algorithm that runs the following   step given a [response](# "subtitle") response: set fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](# "subtitle") to response.   
       
3.         Let foundPreloadedResource be the result of invoking   [consume a preloaded resource](# "subtitle") for request’s [client](# "subtitle"), given   request’s [URL](# "subtitle"), request’s [destination](# "subtitle"),   request’s [mode](# "subtitle"), request’s   [credentials mode](# "subtitle"), request’s [integrity metadata](# "subtitle"),   and onPreloadedResponseAvailable.   
       
4.         If foundPreloadedResource is true and fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](# "subtitle") is null, then set fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](# "subtitle") to "`pending`".  
      

     
-       
If request’s [header list](# "subtitle")  [does not contain](# "subtitle") \``Accept`\`, 
then:  
     

       1.         Let value be \``*/*`\`.   
       
2.         If request’s [initiator](# "subtitle") is "`prefetch`", then set   value to the [document \``Accept`\` header value](# "subtitle").   
       
3.         Otherwise, the user agent should set value to the first matching statement, if    any, switching on request’s [destination](# "subtitle"):    
                 "`document`"              "`frame`"              "`iframe`"              the [document \``Accept`\` header value](# "subtitle")              "`image`"              \``image/png,image/svg+xml,image/*;q=0.8,*/*;q=0.5`\`              "`json`"              \``application/json,*/*;q=0.5`\`              "`style`"              \``text/css,*/*;q=0.1`\`              "`text`"              \``text/plain,*/*;q=0.5`\`                   
4.         [Append](# "subtitle") (\``Accept`\`, value) to   request’s [header list](# "subtitle").  
      

     
-       
If request’s [header list](# "subtitle")  [does not contain](# "subtitle") \``Accept-Language`\` 
and request’s  [client](# "subtitle") is non-null:  
     

       1.         Let emulatedLanguage be the [WebDriver BiDi emulated language](# "subtitle") for   request’s [client](# "subtitle").   
       
2.         If emulatedLanguage is non-null:   
       

         1.           Let encodedEmulatedLanguage be emulatedLanguage,    [isomorphic encoded](# "subtitle").    
         
2.           [Append](# "subtitle")    (\``Accept-Language`\`, encodedEmulatedLanguage) to request’s    [header list](# "subtitle").   
        

      

     
-       
If request’s [header list](# "subtitle") [does not contain](# "subtitle") \``Accept-Language`\`, 
then user agents should [append](# "subtitle") (\``Accept-Language`, an appropriate [header value](# "subtitle")) to
request’s [header list](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If request’s [internal priority](# "subtitle") is null, then use  request’s [priority](# "subtitle"), [initiator](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"),  [destination](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), and [render-blocking](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") in an  [implementation-defined](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") manner to set
request’s  [internal priority](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") to an [implementation-defined](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") object.  
      
The [implementation-defined](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") object could encompass stream weight and  dependency for HTTP/2, priorities used in
*Extensible Prioritization Scheme for HTTP*  for transports where it applies (including 
HTTP/3), and equivalent information used to prioritize  dispatch and processing of 
HTTP/1 fetches. [[RFC9218]](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") 
     
-       
If request is a [subresource request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"):  
     

       1.         Let record be a new [fetch record](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") whose   [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is request and [controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")   is fetchParams’s [controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").   
       
2.         [Append](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") record to request’s   [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [fetch group](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s   [fetch records](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").  
      

     
-       
Run [main fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") given fetchParams. 

     
-       
Return fetchParams’s [controller](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").
    

      

    
To *populate request from client* given a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")
request:
   

     1.       If request’s [traversable for user prompts](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is "`client`":  
     

       1.         Set request’s [traversable for user prompts](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") to   "`no-traversable`".   
       
2.         If request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is non-null:    
       

         1.           Let global be request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s     [global object](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").     
         
2.           If global is a `[Window](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")` object and global’s     [navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is not null, then set request’s     [traversable for user prompts](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") to global’s     [navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s [traversable navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").    
        

      

     
-       If request’s [origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is "`client`":  
     

       1.         [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"): request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is non-null.   
       
2.         Set request’s [origin](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") to request’s   [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  
      

     
-       If request’s [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`client`":  
     

       1.         If request’s [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is non-null, then set   request’s [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to a   [clone](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of request’s [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s   [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   
       
2.         Otherwise, set request’s [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to a new   [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  
      

    

      
### 4.1. Main fetch
   

    
To *main fetch*, given a [fetch params](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")fetchParams 
and an optional boolean recursive (default false), run thesesteps:
   

     1.       Let request be fetchParams’s [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let response be null. 
     
3.       If request’s [local-URLs-only flag](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is set and request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not [local](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set response to a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Run [report Content Security Policy violations for request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
5.       [Upgrade request to a potentially trustworthy URL, if appropriate](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
6.       [Upgrade a mixed content request to a potentially trustworthy URL, if appropriate](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
7.       If [should request be blocked due to a bad port](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [should fetching request be blocked as mixed content](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [should request be blocked by Content Security Policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), or [should request be blocked by Integrity Policy Policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") returns **blocked**, then set response to a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
8.       If request’s [referrer policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is the empty string, then set request’s [referrer policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to request’s [policy container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [referrer policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
9.       If request’s [referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not "`no-referrer`", then set  request’s [referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to the result of invoking  [determine request’s referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). [[REFERRER]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")  
      
	As stated in *Referrer Policy*, user agents can provide the end user with  options to override request’s [referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to "`no-referrer`"  or have it expose less sensitive information. 
     
10.       Set request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to  "`https`" if all of the following conditions are true:  
             11. request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is   "`http`"          
12. request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is a   [domain](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")          
13. request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s   [public suffix](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not "`localhost`" or "`localhost.`"          
14. Matching request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") per   [Known HSTS Host Domain Name Matching](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   results in either a superdomain match with an asserted `includeSubDomains` directive   or a congruent match (with or without an asserted `includeSubDomains` directive) [[HSTS]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"); or   DNS resolution for the request finds a matching HTTPS RR per   [section 9.5](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   of [[SVCB]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   [[HSTS]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [[SVCB]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")        
      
As all DNS operations are generally [implementation-defined](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), how it is  determined that DNS resolution contains an HTTPS RR is also [implementation-defined](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle"). As DNS  operations are not traditionally performed until attempting to [obtain a connection](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key"), user  agents might need to perform DNS operations earlier, consult local DNS caches, or wait until later  in the fetch algorithm and potentially unwind logic on discovering the need to change 
request’s [current URL](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key")’s [scheme](/commit-snapshots/394d20d144ed1401c2c0e02c35bc3608cb2a2269/ "You can also press the 'y' key"). 
     
15.       
If recursive is false, then run the remaining steps [in parallel](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese"). 
     
16.       
If response is null, then set response to the result of running the steps  corresponding 
to the first matching statement:  
             fetchParams’s [preloaded response candidate](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese") is non-null           
      

         1.           Wait until fetchParams’s     [preloaded response candidate](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`pending`".     
         
2.           [Assert](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3"): fetchParams’s     [preloaded response candidate](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a [response](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3").     
         
3.           Return fetchParams’s [preloaded response candidate](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3").    
        

       request’s [current URL](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [origin](#methods "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   [same origin](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3") with
request’s [origin](#bodies "Content Security Policy Level 3"), and request’s 
  [response tainting](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`basic`"       
   request’s [current URL](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [scheme](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") is  
"`data`"          request’s [mode](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   "`navigate`", 
"`websocket`" or "`webtransport`"                 

         1.           Set request’s     [response tainting](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3") to "`basic`".     
         
2.           Return the result of running [override fetch](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") given "`scheme-fetch`"     and fetchParams.    
        

        
HTML assigns any documents and workers created from [URLs](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose    [scheme](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`data`" a unique [opaque origin](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3"). Service workers can    only be created from [URLs](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose [scheme](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [HTTP(S) scheme](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3").    [[HTML]](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") [[SW]](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3")   
       request’s [mode](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`same-origin`" 
                 
Return a [network error](#http-requests "Content Security Policy Level 3").   
       request’s [mode](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`no-cors`" 
                

         1.           If request’s [redirect mode](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`follow`",     then return a [network error](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3").     
         
2.           Set request’s [response tainting](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3") to "`opaque`".     
         
3.           Return the result of running [override fetch](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") given "`scheme-fetch`" and     fetchParams.    
        

       request’s [current URL](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [scheme](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not an   [HTTP(S) scheme](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")           
       
Return a [network error](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3").   
       request’s [use-CORS-preflight flag](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is set         
request’s [unsafe-request flag](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") is set and either
request’s   [method](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not a [CORS-safelisted method](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") or   [CORS-unsafe request-header names](#http-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") with
request’s [header list](#http-redirect-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")   [is not empty](#http-network-or-cache-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")           
      

         1.           Set request’s     [response tainting](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") to     "`cors`".     
         
2.           Let corsWithPreflightResponse be the result of running [override fetch](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3")     given "`http-fetch`", fetchParams, and true.     
         
3.           If corsWithPreflightResponse is a [network error](#deferred-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then     [clear cache entries](#deferred-fetch-quota "Content Security Policy Level 3") using request.     
         
4.           Return corsWithPreflightResponse.    
        

       Otherwise                 

         1.           Set request’s     [response tainting](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3") to     "`cors`".     
         
2.           Return the result of running [override fetch](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") given "`http-fetch`" and     fetchParams.    
        

           
-       
If recursive is true, then return response. 
     
-       
If response is not a [network error](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3") and
response is not a  [filtered response](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then:  
     

       1.         If request’s [response tainting](#background-reading "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`cors`", then:    
       

         1.           Let headerNames be the result of [extracting header list values](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3") given     \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` and response’s     [header list](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3").     
         
2.           If request’s [credentials mode](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not     "`include`" and headerNames contains \``*`\`, then set     response’s [CORS-exposed header-name list](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") to all unique     [header](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") [names](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") in response’s     [header list](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3").     
         
3.           Otherwise, if headerNames is non-null or failure, then set response’s      [CORS-exposed header-name list](#index-defined-here "Content Security Policy Level 3") to headerNames.      
          
	One of the headerNames can still be \``*`\` at this point,      but will only match a [header](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose [name](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") is \``*`\`.    
        

       
-         Set response to the following [filtered response](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3") with response as    its [internal response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3"), depending on request’s    [response tainting](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3"):    
                 "`basic`"              [basic filtered response](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Content Security Policy Level 3")              "`cors`"              [CORS filtered response](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly")              "`opaque`"              [opaque filtered response](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests")                  

     
-       
Let internalResponse be response, if response is a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css); otherwise
response’s [internal response](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If internalResponse’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle") [is empty](h-entry "subtitle"), then  set it to a [clone](h-entry "subtitle") of
request’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle").  
      
A [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [URL list](h-entry "subtitle") can be empty, e.g., when  fetching an `about:` 
URL. 
     
-       
Set internalResponse’s [redirect taint](h-entry "subtitle") to request’s [redirect-taint](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If request is a [navigation request](h-entry "subtitle"), then set internalResponse’s 
[navigation timing allow values list](h-entry "subtitle") to a [clone](h-entry "subtitle") of
request’s [navigation timing allow values list](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If request’s [timing allow failed flag](h-entry "subtitle") is unset, then set
internalResponse’s [timing allow passed flag](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If response is not a [network error](h-entry "subtitle") and any of the following returns  **blocked** 
 
      
       -         [should internalResponse to request be blocked as mixed content](h-entry "subtitle")   
       
-         [should internalResponse to request be blocked by Content Security Policy](h-entry "subtitle")   
       
-         [should internalResponse to request be blocked due to its MIME type](h-entry "subtitle")   
       
-         [should internalResponse to request be blocked due to nosniff](h-entry "subtitle")  
      
      
then set response and internalResponse to a [network error](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
-       
If response’s [type](h-entry "subtitle") is "`opaque`",  internalResponse’s [status](h-entry "subtitle") is a [range status](h-entry "subtitle"), 
internalResponse’s [range-requested flag](h-entry "subtitle") is set, and  request’s 
[header list](h-entry "subtitle") [does not contain](h-entry "subtitle")  \``Range`\`, 
then set response and internalResponse to a  [network error](h-entry "subtitle").  
      

       
Traditionally, APIs accept a ranged response even if a range was not requested. This 
prevents   a partial response or a range not satisfiable response from an earlier 
ranged request being   provided to an API that did not make a range request.   
               Further details        
The above steps prevent the following attack:    
        
A media element is used to request a range of a cross-origin HTML resource. Although 
this is    invalid media, a reference to a clone of the response can be retained 
in a service worker. This    can later be used as the response to a script element’s 
fetch. If the partial response is valid    JavaScript (even though the whole resource 
is not), executing it would leak private data.   
                  
-       
If response is not a [network error](h-entry "subtitle") and  either request’s 
[method](h-entry "subtitle") is  \``HEAD`\` or \``CONNECT`\`, or internalResponse’s  [status](h-entry "subtitle") is a [null body status](h-entry "subtitle"),  set
internalResponse’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") to  null and disregard any enqueuing toward it (if any).  
      
This standardizes the error handling for servers that violate HTTP. 
     
-       
If request’s [integrity metadata](h-entry "subtitle") is not the empty string, then:  
     

       1.         Let processBodyError be this step: run [fetch response handover](h-entry "subtitle") given   fetchParams and a [network error](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
2.         If response’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") is null, then run   processBodyError and abort these steps.   
       
3.         Let processBody given bytes be these steps:    
       

         1.           If bytes do not [match](h-entry "subtitle")     request’s [integrity metadata](h-entry "subtitle"), then run     processBodyError and abort these steps. [[SRI]](h-entry "subtitle")     
         
2.           Set response’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") to bytes     [as a body](h-entry "subtitle").     
         
3.           Run [fetch response handover](h-entry "subtitle") given fetchParams and response.    
        

       
-         [Fully read](h-entry "subtitle") response’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") given   processBody and processBodyError.  
      

     
-       
Otherwise, run [fetch response handover](h-entry "subtitle") given fetchParams and 
response.
    

      
---
   

    
The *fetch response handover*, given a [fetch params](h-entry "subtitle")fetchParams 
and a [response](h-entry "subtitle") response, run these steps:
   

     1.       Let timingInfo be fetchParams’s [timing info](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If response is not a [network error](h-entry "subtitle")  and fetchParams’s  [request](h-entry "subtitle")’s [client](h-entry "subtitle") is a [secure context](h-entry "subtitle"), then set  timingInfo’s [server-timing headers](h-entry "subtitle") to the  result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](h-entry "subtitle") \``Server-Timing`\`  from response’s [internal response](h-entry "subtitle")’s  [header list](h-entry "subtitle").  
      
	Using _response_’s [internal response](h-entry "subtitle") is safe as  exposing \``Server-Timing`\` header data is guarded through the  \``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` header.  
      
	The user agent may decide to expose \``Server-Timing`\` headers to non-secure contexts  requests as well. 
     
3.       If fetchParams’s [request](h-entry "subtitle")’s [destination](h-entry "subtitle") is "`document`", then set fetchParams’s [controller](h-entry "subtitle")’s [full timing info](h-entry "subtitle") to fetchParams’s [timing info](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Let processResponseEndOfBody be the following steps:  
     

       1.         Let unsafeEndTime be the [unsafe shared current time](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
2.         Set fetchParams’s [controller](h-entry "subtitle")’s    [report timing steps](h-entry "subtitle") to the following steps given a    [global object](h-entry "subtitle") global:    
       

         1.           If fetchParams’s [request](h-entry "subtitle")’s [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s     [scheme](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is not an [HTTP(S) scheme](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), then return.     
         
2.           Set timingInfo’s [end time](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to the     [relative high resolution time](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given unsafeEndTime and     global.     
         
3.           Let cacheState be response’s [cache state](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").     
         
4.           Let bodyInfo be response’s [body info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").     
         
5.           If response’s [timing allow passed flag](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is not set,      then set timingInfo to the result of [creating an opaque timing info](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") for      timingInfo and set cacheState to the empty string.      
          
	This covers the case of response being a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").     
         
6.           Let responseStatus be 0.     
         
7.           If fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is      not "`navigate`" or response’s      [redirect taint](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is "`same-origin`":      
         

           1.             Set responseStatus to response’s [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").       
           
2.             Let mimeType be the result of       [extracting a MIME type](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") from response’s       [header list](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").       
           
3.             If mimeType is not failure, then set bodyInfo’s       [content type](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to the result of       [minimizing a supported MIME type](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given mimeType.      
          

         
-           If fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s     [initiator type](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is non-null, then [mark resource timing](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given     timingInfo, fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s     [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s     [initiator type](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), global, cacheState,     bodyInfo, and responseStatus.    
        

       
-         Let processResponseEndOfBodyTask be the following steps:    
       

         1.           Set fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s     [done flag](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").     
         
2.           If fetchParams’s [process response end-of-body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is     non-null, then run fetchParams’s     [process response end-of-body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given response.     
         
3.           If fetchParams’s [request](# "subtitle")’s     [initiator type](# "subtitle") is non-null and fetchParams’s     [request](# "subtitle")’s [client](# "subtitle")’s     [global object](# "subtitle") is fetchParams’s     [task destination](# "subtitle"), then run fetchParams’s     [controller](# "subtitle")’s [report timing steps](# "subtitle") given     fetchParams’s [request](# "subtitle")’s [client](# "subtitle")’s     [global object](# "subtitle").    
        

       
-         [Queue a fetch task](# "subtitle") to run processResponseEndOfBodyTask with   fetchParams’s [task destination](# "subtitle").  
      

     
-       If fetchParams’s [process response](# "subtitle") is non-null, then [queue a fetch task](# "subtitle") to run fetchParams’s [process response](# "subtitle") given response, with fetchParams’s [task destination](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       Let internalResponse be response, if response is a [network error](# "subtitle"); otherwise response’s [internal response](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       If response is a [network error](# "subtitle"), then run the [WebDriver BiDi fetch error](# "subtitle") steps with request. Otherwise, run the [WebDriver BiDi response completed](# "subtitle") steps with request and response. 
     
-       If internalResponse’s [body](# "subtitle") is null, then run processResponseEndOfBody. 
     
-       Otherwise:
     

       1.         Let transformStream be a new `[TransformStream](# "subtitle")`.   
       
2.         Let identityTransformAlgorithm be an algorithm which, given chunk,   [enqueues](# "subtitle") chunk in transformStream.   
       
3.         [Set up](# "subtitle") transformStream with   [*transformAlgorithm*](# "subtitle") set to   identityTransformAlgorithm and   [*flushAlgorithm*](# "subtitle") set to   processResponseEndOfBody.   
       
4.         Set internalResponse’s [body](# "subtitle")’s [stream](# "subtitle") to the   result of internalResponse’s [body](# "subtitle")’s [stream](# "subtitle")   [piped through](# "subtitle") transformStream.  
      

      This `[TransformStream](# "subtitle")` is needed for the purpose of receiving a notification when  the stream reaches its end, and is otherwise an [identity transform stream](# "subtitle"). 
     
-       If fetchParams’s [process response consume body](# "subtitle") is  non-null, then:  
     

       1.         Let processBody given nullOrBytes be this step: run   fetchParams’s [process response consume body](# "subtitle") given   response and nullOrBytes.   
       
2.         Let processBodyError be this step: run fetchParams’s   [process response consume body](# "subtitle") given response and failure.   
       
3.         If internalResponse’s [body](# "subtitle") is null, then   [queue a fetch task](# "subtitle") to run processBody given null, with fetchParams’s   [task destination](# "subtitle").   
       
4.         Otherwise, [fully read](# "subtitle") internalResponse’s   [body](# "subtitle") given processBody, processBodyError, and   fetchParams’s [task destination](# "subtitle").  
      

    

      
### 4.2. Override fetch
   

    
To *override fetch*, given "`scheme-fetch`" or"`http-fetch`" type, a [fetch params](# "subtitle") fetchParams, 
andan optional boolean makeCORSPreflight (default false):
   

     1.       Let request be fetchParams’ [request](# "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Let response be the result of executing [potentially override response for a request](# "subtitle") on request. 
     
3.       If response is non-null, then return response. 
     
4.       Switch on type and run the associated step:  
             "`scheme fetch`"                  Set response be the result of running [scheme fetch](# "subtitle") given   fetchParams.   
       "`HTTP fetch`"                  Set response be the result of running [HTTP fetch](# "subtitle") given   fetchParams and makeCORSPreflight.  
           
5.       Return response.
    

      

    
The *potentially override response for a request* algorithm takes a [request](# "subtitle")request, 
and returns either a [response](# "subtitle") or null. Its behavior is[implementation-defined](# "subtitle"), allowing user agents to intervene on the [request](# "subtitle") byreturning a response directly, or allowing the request to proceed by returning null.
    
By default, the algorithm has the following trivial implementation:
   

     1.       Return null.
    

    

     
User agents will generally override this default implementation with a somewhat more 
complex set of behaviors. For example, a user agent might decide that its users' 
safety is best preserved by generally blocking requests to \`https://unsafe.example/\`, 
while synthesizing a shim for the widely-used resource \`https://unsafe.example/widget.js\` 
to avoid breakage. That implementation might look like the following: 
    

      1.        If request’s [current url](# "subtitle")’s [host](# "subtitle")’s  [registrable domain](# "subtitle") is "`unsafe.example`":  
      

        1.          If request’s [current url](# "subtitle")’s [path](# "subtitle") is   « "`widget.js`" »:    
        

          1.            Let body be [*insert a byte sequence representing the shimmed      content here*].     
          
2.            Return a new [response](# "subtitle") with the following properties:      
                       [type](# "subtitle")                   "`cors`"                   [status](# "subtitle")                   200            ...                   ...                   [body](# "subtitle")                   The result of getting body [as a body](# "subtitle").                          

        
-          Return a [network error](# "subtitle").   
       

      
-        Return null. 
     

          
### 4.3. Scheme fetch
   

    
To *scheme fetch*, given a[fetch params](# "subtitle") fetchParams:
   

     1.       If fetchParams is [canceled](# "subtitle"), then return the [appropriate network error](# "subtitle") for fetchParams. 
     
2.       Let request be fetchParams’s [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Switch on request’s [current URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") and run  the associated steps:  
             "`about`"                  If request’s [current URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [path](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is the string    "`blank`", then return a new [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") whose    [status message](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is \``OK`\`, [header list](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is «    (\``Content-Type`\`, \``text/html;charset=utf-8`\`) », and    [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is the empty byte sequence [as a body](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").    
        
	[URLs](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") such as "`about:config`" are handled during    [navigation](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") and result in a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") in the context of    [fetching](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").   
       "`blob`"                 

         1.           Let blobURLEntry be request’s [current URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s     [blob URL entry](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").     
         
2.           If request’s [method](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is not \``GET`\` or      blobURLEntry is null, then return a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). [[FILEAPI]](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")      
          
	The \``GET`\` [method](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") restriction serves no useful purpose      other than being interoperable.     
         
3.           Let requestEnvironment be the result of     [determining the environment](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") given request.     
         
4.           Let isTopLevelSelfFetch be false.     
         
5.           If request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is non-null:      
         

           1.             Let global be request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s       [global object](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").       
           
2.             If all of the following conditions are true:        
                         3.               global is a `[Window](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")` object;         
             
4.               global’s [navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is not null;         
             
5.               global’s [navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s [parent](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")         is null; and         
             
6.               requestEnvironment’s [creation URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")         [equals](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") request’s [current URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"),        
            
            then set isTopLevelSelfFetch to true.      
          

         
-           Let stringOrEnvironment be the result of these steps:      
         

           1.             If request’s [destination](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is "`document`",       then return "`top-level-navigation`".       
           
2.             If isTopLevelSelfFetch is true, then return       "`top-level-self-fetch`".       
           
3.             Return requestEnvironment.      
          

         
-           Let blob be the result of [obtaining a blob object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given     blobURLEntry and stringOrEnvironment.     
         
-           If blob is not a `[Blob](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object, then return a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").     
         
-           Let response be a new [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").     
         
-           Let fullLength be blob’s `[size](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.
