A `[Response](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object has an associated*response* 
(a[response](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3")).
   
A `[Response](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object also has an associated *headers* 
(null or a`[Headers](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object), 
initially null.
   
A `[Response](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object’s [body](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") is its[response](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [body](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3").
   
---
       `response = new [Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")(body = null [, init])`          
Creates a `[Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")` whose body is body, 
and status, status message, and headers are provided by init. 
    `response = [Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") . [error](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers")()` 
         
Creates network error `[Response](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly")`. 
    `response = [Response](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Mixed Content") . [redirect](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests")(url, status = 302)` 
         
Creates a redirect `[Response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css)` 
that redirects to url with status status. 
    `response = [Response](h-entry "subtitle") . [json](h-entry "subtitle")(data [, init])`          
Creates a `[Response](h-entry "subtitle")` whose body is the JSON-encoded data, and status, 
status message, and headers are provided by init. 
    `response . [type](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns response’s type, e.g., "`cors`". 
    `response . [url](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns response’s URL, if it has one; otherwise the empty string. 
    `response . [redirected](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns whether response was obtained through a redirect. 
    `response . [status](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns response’s status. 
    `response . [ok](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns whether response’s status is an [ok status](h-entry "subtitle"). 
    `response . [statusText](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns response’s status message. 
    `response . [headers](h-entry "subtitle")`          
Returns response’s headers as `[Headers](h-entry "subtitle")`. 
    `response . [clone](h-entry "subtitle")()`          
Returns a clone of response.
      
---
   

    
To *create* a `[Response](h-entry "subtitle")` object, given a[response](h-entry "subtitle") response, 
[headers guard](h-entry "subtitle") guard, and[realm](h-entry "subtitle") realm, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       Let responseObject be a [new](h-entry "subtitle") `[Response](h-entry "subtitle")` object with realm. 
     
2.       Set responseObject’s [response](h-entry "subtitle") to response. 
     
3.       Set responseObject’s [headers](h-entry "subtitle") to a [new](h-entry "subtitle") `[Headers](h-entry "subtitle")` object with realm, whose [headers list](h-entry "subtitle") is response’s [headers list](h-entry "subtitle") and [guard](h-entry "subtitle") is guard. 
     
4.       Return responseObject.
    

      

    
To *initialize a response*, given a `[Response](h-entry "subtitle")` object response,`[ResponseInit](h-entry "subtitle")` 
init, and null or a [body with type](h-entry "subtitle") body:
   

     1.       If init["`[status](h-entry "subtitle")`"] is not in the range 200 to 599, inclusive, then [throw](h-entry "subtitle") a `[RangeError](h-entry "subtitle")`. 
     
2.       If init["`[statusText](h-entry "subtitle")`"] is not the empty string and does not match the [reason-phrase](h-entry "subtitle") token production, then [throw](h-entry "subtitle") a `[TypeError](h-entry "subtitle")`. 
     
3.       Set response’s [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [status](h-entry "subtitle") to init["`[status](h-entry "subtitle")`"]. 
     
4.       Set response’s [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [status message](h-entry "subtitle") to init["`[statusText](h-entry "subtitle")`"]. 
     
5.       If init["`[headers](h-entry "subtitle")`"] [exists](h-entry "subtitle"), then [fill](h-entry "subtitle") response’s [headers](h-entry "subtitle") with init["`[headers](h-entry "subtitle")`"]. 
     
6.       If body is non-null, then:  
     

       1.         If response’s [status](h-entry "subtitle") is a [null body status](h-entry "subtitle"), then    [throw](h-entry "subtitle") a `[TypeError](h-entry "subtitle")`.    
        
	101 and 103 are included in [null body status](h-entry "subtitle") due to their use elsewhere.    They do not affect this step.   
       
2.         Set response’s [body](h-entry "subtitle") to body’s   [body](h-entry "subtitle").   
       
3.         If body’s [type](h-entry "subtitle") is non-null and   response’s [header list](h-entry "subtitle") [does not contain](h-entry "subtitle")   \``Content-Type`\`, then [append](h-entry "subtitle") (\``Content-Type`\`,   body’s [type](h-entry "subtitle")) to response’s   [header list](h-entry "subtitle").  
      

    

      
---
   

    
The*`new Response(body, init)`*constructor steps are:
   

     1.       Set [this](h-entry "subtitle")’s [response](h-entry "subtitle") to a new [response](h-entry "subtitle"). 
     
2.       Set [this](h-entry "subtitle")’s [headers](h-entry "subtitle") to a [new](h-entry "subtitle") `[Headers](h-entry "subtitle")` object with [this](h-entry "subtitle")’s [relevant realm](h-entry "subtitle"), whose [header list](h-entry "subtitle") is [this](h-entry "subtitle")’s [response](h-entry "subtitle")’s [header list](h-entry "subtitle") and [guard](h-entry "subtitle") is "`response`". 
     
3.       Let bodyWithType be null. 
     
4.       If body is non-null, then set bodyWithType to the result of [extracting](h-entry "subtitle") body. 
     
5.       Perform [initialize a response](h-entry "subtitle") given [this](h-entry "subtitle"), init, and bodyWithType.
    

      
The static *`error()`* method steps are to return theresult of [creating](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") a `[Response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")` 
object, given a new [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"),"`immutable`", 
and the [current realm](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").
   

    
The static*`redirect(url, status)`* method stepsare:
   

     1.       Let parsedURL be the result of [parsing](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") url with [current settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [API base URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
2.       If parsedURL is failure, then [throw](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") a `[TypeError](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`. 
     
3.       If status is not a [redirect status](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), then [throw](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") a `[RangeError](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`. 
     
4.       Let responseObject be the result of [creating](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") a `[Response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")` object, given a new [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), "`immutable`", and the [current realm](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
5.       Set responseObject’s [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to status. 
     
6.       Let value be parsedURL, [serialized](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") and [isomorphic encoded](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
7.       [Append](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") (\``Location`\`, value) to responseObject’s [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [header list](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
8.       Return responseObject.
    

      

    
The static*`json(data, init)`* method stepsare:
   

     1.       Let bytes the result of running [serialize a JavaScript value to JSON bytes](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") on data. 
     
2.       Let body be the result of [extracting](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") bytes. 
     
3.       Let responseObject be the result of [creating](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") a `[Response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")` object, given a new [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), "`response`", and the [current realm](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Perform [initialize a response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") given responseObject, init, and (body, "`application/json`"). 
     
5.       Return responseObject.
    

      
The *`type`* getter steps are to return [this](# "subtitle")’s[response](# "subtitle")’s [type](# "subtitle").
   
The *`url`* getter steps are to returnthe empty string if [this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [URL](# "subtitle") is null;otherwise [this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [URL](# "subtitle"),[serialized](# "subtitle") with
[*exclude fragment*](# "subtitle") setto true.
   
The *`redirected`* getter steps are to return true if[this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [URL list](# "subtitle")’s [size](# "subtitle") isgreater than 1; otherwise false.
   
To filter out [responses](# "subtitle") that are the result of aredirect, do this directly through the API, e.g., `fetch(url, { redirect:"error"
})`.This way a potentially unsafe [response](# "subtitle") cannot accidentally leak.
   
The *`status`* getter steps are to return[this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [status](# "subtitle").
   
The *`ok`* getter steps are to return true if[this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [status](# "subtitle") is an [ok status](# "subtitle");otherwise false.
   
The *`statusText`* getter steps are to return[this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle")’s [status message](# "subtitle").
   
The *`headers`* getter steps are to return[this](# "subtitle")’s [headers](# "subtitle").
   
---
   

    
The *`clone()`* method steps are:
   

     1.       If [this](# "subtitle") is [unusable](# "subtitle"), then [throw](# "subtitle") a `[TypeError](# "subtitle")`. 
     
2.       Let clonedResponse be the result of [cloning](# "subtitle") [this](# "subtitle")’s [response](# "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Return the result of [creating](# "subtitle") a `[Response](# "subtitle")` object, given clonedResponse, [this](# "subtitle")’s [headers](# "subtitle")’s [guard](# "subtitle"), and [this](# "subtitle")’s [relevant realm](# "subtitle").
    

      
### 5.6. Fetch methods

```partial interface mixin [WindowOrWorkerGlobalScope](# "subtitle") {  [[NewObject](# "subtitle")]
[Promise](# "subtitle")<[Response](# "subtitle")> [fetch](# "subtitle")([RequestInfo](# "subtitle") *h-entry status-LS
input*, optional [RequestInit](# "subtitle") *h-entry status-LS
init* = {});};dictionary *h-entry status-LS
DeferredRequestInit* : [RequestInit](# "subtitle") {  [DOMHighResTimeStamp](# "subtitle") *h-entry status-LS
activateAfter*;};[[Exposed](# "subtitle")=Window]interface *h-entry status-LS
FetchLaterResult* {  readonly attribute [boolean](# "subtitle") [activated](# "subtitle");};partial
interface [Window](# "subtitle") {  [[NewObject](# "subtitle"), [SecureContext](# "subtitle")]
[FetchLaterResult](# "subtitle") [fetchLater](# "subtitle")([RequestInfo](# "subtitle") *h-entry status-LS
input*, optional [DeferredRequestInit](# "subtitle") *h-entry status-LS
init* = {});};```


The*`fetch(input, init)`*method steps are:


1.       Let p be [a new promise](# "subtitle").

2.       Let requestObject be the result of invoking the initial value of `[Request](# "subtitle")` as constructor with input and init as arguments. If this throws an exception, [reject](# "subtitle") p with it and return p.

3.       Let request be requestObject’s [request](# "subtitle").

4.       If requestObject’s [signal](# "subtitle") is [aborted](# "subtitle"),  then:  


1.         [Abort the `fetch()` call](# "subtitle") with p, request, null, and   requestObject’s [signal](# "subtitle")’s [abort reason](# "subtitle").   

2.         Return p.  


- Let globalObject be  request’s [client](# "subtitle")’s [global object](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").      
- If globalObject is a `[ServiceWorkerGlobalScope](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")` object, then set request’s [service-workers mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") to "`none`".      
-       Let responseObject be null.

-       Let relevantRealm be [this](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [relevant realm](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").

-       Let locallyAborted be false.  

	This lets us reject promises with predictable timing, when the request to abort  comes from the same thread as the call to fetch.

-       Let controller be null.

-       [Add the following abort steps](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") to requestObject’s  [signal](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"):  


1.         Set locallyAborted to true.   

2.         [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"): controller is non-null.   

3.         [Abort](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") controller with requestObject’s   [signal](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [abort reason](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").   

4.         [Abort the `fetch()` call](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") with p, request,   responseObject, and requestObject’s [signal](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s   [abort reason](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").  


-       

	Set controller to the result of calling [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") given  request and [*processResponse*](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") given response being  these steps:  


1.         If locallyAborted is true, then abort these steps.   

2.         If response’s [aborted flag](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is set, then:    


1.           Let deserializedError be the result of     [deserialize a serialized abort reason](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") given controller’s     [serialized abort reason](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") and relevantRealm.     

2.           [Abort the `fetch()` call](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") with p, request,     responseObject, and deserializedError.     

3.           Abort these steps.    


-         If response is a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle"), then [reject](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") p   with a `[TypeError](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")` and abort these steps.   

-         Set responseObject to the result of [creating](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") a `[Response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")`   object, given response, "`immutable`", and relevantRealm.   

-         [Resolve](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") p with responseObject.  


-       Return p.


To *abort a `fetch()` call*with a promise, request, responseObject, and an error:


1.       [Reject](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") promise with error.  

	This is a no-op if promise has already fulfilled.

2.       If request’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is non-null and is [readable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then [cancel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") request’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with error.

3.       If responseObject is null, then return.

4.       Let response be responseObject’s [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

5.       If response’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is non-null and is [readable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then [error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with error.


A `[FetchLaterResult](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` has an associated *activated getter steps*,which
is an algorithm returning a boolean.


The *`activated`* getter steps are to returnthe result of running [this](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [activated getter steps](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


The *`fetchLater(input, init)`*method steps are:


1.       Let requestObject be the result of invoking the initial value of `[Request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` as constructor with input and init as arguments.

2.       If requestObject’s [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is [aborted](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [abort reason](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

3.       Let request be requestObject’s [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

4.       Let activateAfter be null.

5.       If init is given and init["`[activateAfter](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"] [exists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set activateAfter to init["`[activateAfter](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"].

6.       If activateAfter is less than 0, then throw a `[RangeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.

7.       If [this](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [relevant global object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [associated document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not [fully active](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.

8.       If request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not an [HTTP(S) scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.

9.       If request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [potentially trustworthy URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.

10.       If request’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not null, and request’s  [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [length](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is null, then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.  

	Requests whose [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is a `[ReadableStream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object cannot be  deferred.

11.       Let quota be the [available deferred-fetch quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request’s [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

12.       Let requested be request’s [total request length](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

13.       If quota is less than requested, then [throw](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") a `[QuotaExceededError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` whose [quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is quota and [requested](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is requested.

14.       Let activated be false.

15.       Let deferredRecord be the result of calling [queue a deferred fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request, activateAfter, and the following step: set activated to true.

16.       [Add the following abort steps](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to requestObject’s [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): Set deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") to "`aborted`".

17.       Return a new `[FetchLaterResult](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key")` whose [activated getter steps](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") are to return activated.


The following call would queue a request to be fetched when the document is terminated:

```p-summary
fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
  method: "POST",
  body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
  headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }
})
```

The following call would also queue this request after 5 seconds, and the returned
value would allow callers to observe if it was indeed activated. Note that the request
is guaranteed to be invoked, even in cases where the user agent throttles timers.

```p-summary
const result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
  method: "POST",
  body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
  headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
  activateAfter: 5000
});

function check_if_fetched() {
  return result.activated;
}
```

The `[FetchLaterResult](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object can be used together
with an `[AbortSignal](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3")`. For example:

```content
let accumulated_events = [];
let previous_result = null;
const abort_signal = new AbortSignal();
function accumulate_event(event) {
  if (previous_result) {
    if (previous_result.activated) {
      // The request is already activated, we can start from scratch.
      accumulated_events = [];
    } else {
      // Abort this request, and start a new one with all the events.
      signal.abort();
    }
  }

  accumulated_events.push(event);
  result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
    method: "POST",
    body: JSON.stringify(accumulated_events),
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    activateAfter: 5000,
    abort_signal
  });
}
```

Any of the following calls to [`fetchLater()`](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") would throw:

```secno
// Only [potentially trustworthy URL](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3")s are supported.
fetchLater("http://untrusted.example.com");

// The length of the deferred request has to be known when.
fetchLater("https://origin.example.com", {body: someDynamicStream});

// Deferred fetching only works on active windows.
const detachedWindow = iframe.contentWindow;
iframe.remove();
detachedWindow.fetchLater("https://origin.example.com");
```

See [deferred fetch quota examples](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") for examples portraying how the deferred-fetch quota works.

### 5.7. Garbage collection

The user agent may [terminate](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3") an ongoing fetch if that terminationis not observable through script.

"Observable through script" means observable through[`fetch()`](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s arguments and return value. Other ways, such as communicatingwith the server through a side-channel are not included.

The server being able to observe garbage collection has precedent, e.g., with`[WebSocket](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3")`
and `[XMLHttpRequest](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")` objects.


The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed.

```content
fetch("https://www.example.com/")
```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because the termination can be observed
through the promise.

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")
```

The user agent can terminate the fetch because the associated body is not observable.

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.headers)
```

The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed.

```content
fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)
```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because one can observe the termination
by registering a handler for the promise object.

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")
  .then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)
```

The user agent cannot terminate the fetch as termination would be observable via
the registered handler.

```secno
fetch("https://www.example.com/")
  .then(res => {
    res.body.getReader().closed.then(() => console.log("stream closed!"))
  })
```

(The above examples of non-observability assume that built-in properties and functions,
such as  `[body.getReader()](#deferred-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")`, have not been overwritten.)

## 6. `data:` URLs

For an informative description of `data:` URLs, see RFC 2397. This section replacesthat
RFC’s normative processing requirements to be compatible with deployed content.
[[RFC2397]](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3")

A *`data:` URL struct* is a [struct](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") that consists of
a*MIME type* (a [MIME type](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3")) and a*body*
(a [byte sequence](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")).


The *`data:` URL processor* takes a [URL](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level
3")dataURL and then runs these steps:


1.       [Assert](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3"): dataURL’s [scheme](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`data`".

2.       Let input be the result of running the [URL serializer](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") on dataURL with [*exclude fragment*](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") set to true.

3.       Remove the leading "`data:`" from input.

4.       Let position point at the start of input.

5.       Let mimeType be the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") that are not equal to U+002C (,), given position.

6.       [Strip leading and trailing ASCII whitespace](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") from mimeType.  

	This will only remove U+0020 SPACE [code points](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3"), if any.

7.       If position is past the end of input, then return failure.

8.       Advance position by 1.

9.       Let encodedBody be the remainder of input.

10.       Let body be the [percent-decoding](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") of encodedBody.

11.       If mimeType ends with U+003B (;), followed by zero or more U+0020 SPACE, followed by  an [ASCII case-insensitive](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for "`base64`", then:  


1.         Let stringBody be the [isomorphic decode](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly") of body.   

2.         Set body to the [forgiving-base64 decode](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests") of stringBody.   

3.         If body is failure, then return failure.   

4.         Remove the last 6 [code points](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) from mimeType.   

5.         Remove trailing U+0020 SPACE [code points](h-entry "subtitle") from mimeType, if any.   

6.         Remove the last U+003B (;) from mimeType.  


-       If mimeType [starts with](h-entry "subtitle") "`;`", then prepend "`text/plain`" to mimeType.

-       Let mimeTypeRecord be the result of [parsing](h-entry "subtitle") mimeType.

-       If mimeTypeRecord is failure, then set mimeTypeRecord to `text/plain;charset=US-ASCII`.

-       Return a new [`data:` URL struct](h-entry "subtitle") whose [MIME type](h-entry "subtitle") is mimeTypeRecord and [body](h-entry "subtitle") is body.


## Background reading

*This section and its subsections are informative only.*

### HTTP header layer division

For the purposes of fetching, there is an API layer (HTML’s `img`, CSS’s`background-image`), early
fetch layer, service worker layer, and network & cachelayer. \``Accept`\` and \``Accept-Language`\`
are set in the early fetch layer(typically by the user agent). Most other headers
controlled by the user agent, such as\``Accept-Encoding`\`, \``Host`\`, and \``Referer`\`, are set in thenetwork
& cache layer. Developers can set headers either at the API layer or in the serviceworker
layer (typically through a `[Request](h-entry "subtitle")` object). Developers have almost
no control over[forbidden request-headers](h-entry "subtitle"), but can control \``Accept`\`
and have the means toconstrain and omit \``Referer`\` for instance.

### Atomic HTTP redirect handling

Redirects (a [response](h-entry "subtitle") whose [status](h-entry "subtitle") or[internal response](h-entry "subtitle")’s (if any) [status](h-entry "subtitle") is a[redirect status](h-entry "subtitle")) are not exposed to APIs. Exposing redirects might leak information nototherwise available through a cross-site scripting attack.

A fetch to `https://example.org/auth` that includes a`Cookie` marked `HttpOnly` could result in a redirect to`https://other-origin.invalid/4af955781ea1c84a3b11`. This new
URL contains asecret. If we expose redirects that secret would be available through
a cross-sitescripting attack.

### Basic safe CORS protocol setup

For resources where data is protected through IP authentication or a firewall(unfortunately
relatively common still), using the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") is**unsafe**.
(This is the reason why the [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") had to beinvented.)

However, otherwise using the following [header](h-entry "subtitle") is**safe**:

```h-entry
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *
```

Even if a resource exposes additional information based on cookie or HTTPauthentication,
using the above [header](h-entry "subtitle") will not revealit. It will share the resource with APIs such as`[XMLHttpRequest](h-entry
"subtitle")`, much like it is already shared with`curl` and `wget`.

Thus in other words, if a resource cannot be accessed from a random device connected
tothe web using `curl` and `wget` the aforementioned[header](h-entry "subtitle") is not to be included. If it can be accessedhowever, it is perfectly fine to do so.

### CORS protocol and HTTP caches

If [CORS protocol](h-entry "subtitle") requirements are more complicated than setting\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` to `*`
or a static[origin](h-entry "subtitle"), \``Vary`\` is to be used.[[HTML]](h-entry "subtitle") [[HTTP]](h-entry "subtitle") [[HTTP-CACHING]](h-entry "subtitle")

```h-entry
Vary: Origin
```

In particular, consider what happens if \``Vary`\` is *not* used and a server isconfigured
to send \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` for a certainresource only in response to a [CORS request](h-entry "subtitle"). When a user agent receives a response to anon-[CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") for that resource (for example, as the result of a [navigationrequest](h-entry "subtitle")), the response will lack \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\`and the user agent will cache that response. Then, if the user agent subsequently encounters a[CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") for the resource, it will use that cached response from the previousnon-[CORS request](h-entry "subtitle"), without \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\`.

But if \``Vary: Origin`\` is used in the same scenario described above, it will causethe user
agent to [fetch](h-entry "subtitle") a response that includes\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\`, rather than using the cached responsefrom the previous non-[CORS request](h-entry "subtitle") that lacks\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\`.

However, if \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` is set to`*` or a static [origin](h-entry "subtitle") for a particular resource, then configure the serverto always send \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](h-entry "subtitle")\` in responses for theresource — for non-[CORS requests](h-entry "subtitle") as well as [CORSrequests](h-entry "subtitle") — and do not use
\``Vary`\`.

### WebSockets

As part of establishing a connection, the `[WebSocket](h-entry "subtitle")` object initiates
a special kind of[fetch](h-entry "subtitle") (using a [request](h-entry "subtitle") whose [mode](h-entry "subtitle")
is"`websocket`") which allows it to share in many fetch policy decisions, such*HTTP
Strict Transport Security (HSTS)*. Ultimately this results in fetch calling into*WebSockets*
to obtain a dedicated connection. [[WEBSOCKETS]](h-entry "subtitle")[[HSTS]](h-entry "subtitle")

Fetch used to define[obtain a WebSocket connection](h-entry "subtitle") and[establish a WebSocket connection](h-entry "subtitle") directly, butboth are now defined in
*WebSockets*. [[WEBSOCKETS]](h-entry "subtitle")

## Using fetch in other standards

In its essence [fetching](h-entry "subtitle") is an exchange of a [request](h-entry "subtitle") for a[response](h-entry "subtitle"). In reality it is rather complex mechanism for standards to adopt and usecorrectly. This section aims to give some advice.

Always ask domain experts for review.

This is a work in progress.

### Setting up a request

The first step in [fetching](h-entry "subtitle") is to create a [request](h-entry "subtitle"), and populate its[items](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").

Start by setting the [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") and [method](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"),as defined by HTTP. If your
\``POST`\` or \``PUT`\` [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") needs abody, you set [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to a [byte sequence](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"), or toa new [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") whose [stream](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is a `[ReadableStream](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`
you created. [[HTTP]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")

Choose your [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [destination](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") using the guidance in the[destination table](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). [Destinations](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")
affect*Content Security Policy* and have other implications such as the \``[Sec-Fetch-Dest](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css
"subtitle")`\`header, so they are much more than informative metadata. If a new
feature requires a[destination](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") that’s not in the [destination table](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"),please[file an issue](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")to discuss. [[CSP]](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")

Set your [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to the[environment settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") you’re operating in. Web-exposed APIs are generally defined withWeb IDL, for which every object that implements an [interface](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") has a[relevant settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") you can use. For example, a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") associated with an[element](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") would set the [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") to the element’s[node document](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [relevant settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). All features that are directly web-exposedby JavaScript, HTML, CSS, or other `[Document](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`
subresources should have a[client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle").

If your [fetching](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is not directly web-exposed, e.g., it is sent in the backgroundwithout relying on a current `[Window](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`
or `[Worker](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")`, leave [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s[client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") as null and set the [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")’s [origin](# "subtitle"),[policy container](# "subtitle"), [service-workers mode](# "subtitle"), and[referrer](# "subtitle") to appropriate values instead, e.g., by copying them from the[environment settings object](# "subtitle") ahead of time. In these more advanced cases, make sure thedetails of how your fetch handles
*Content Security Policy* and[referrer policy](# "subtitle") are fleshed out. Also make sure you handle concurrency, as callbacks(see [Invoking fetch and processing responses](# "subtitle")) would be posted on a [parallel queue](# "subtitle"). [[REFERRER]](# "subtitle") [[CSP]](# "subtitle")

Think through the way you intend to handle cross-origin resources. Some features
may only work inthe [same origin](# "subtitle"), in which case set your [request](# "subtitle")’s [mode](# "subtitle")
to"`same-origin`". Otherwise, new web-exposed features should almost always set
their[mode](# "subtitle") to "`cors`". If your feature is not web-exposed, or you thinkthere
is another reason for it to fetch cross-origin resources without CORS, please[file an issue](# "subtitle")to discuss.

For cross-origin requests, also determines if [credentials](# "subtitle") are to be included withthe requests, in which case set your [request](# "subtitle")’s [credentials mode](# "subtitle")
to"`include`".

Figure out if your fetch needs to be reported to *Resource Timing*, and with which[initiator type](# "subtitle"). By passing an [initiator type](# "subtitle") to the[request](# "subtitle"), reporting to
*Resource Timing* will be done automatically once thefetch is done and the [response](# "subtitle") is fully downloaded. [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](# "subtitle")

If your request requires additional HTTP headers, set its [header list](# "subtitle") toa [header list](# "subtitle") that contains those headers, e.g., «
(\``My-Header-Name`\`,\``My-Header-Value`\`) ». Sending custom headers may have implications,
such as requiring a[CORS-preflight fetch](# "subtitle"), so handle with care.

If you want to override the default caching mechanism, e.g., disable caching for
this[request](# "subtitle"), set the request’s [cache mode](# "subtitle") to a value other
than"`default`".

Determine whether you want your request to support redirects. If you don’t, set
its[redirect mode](# "subtitle") to "`error`".

Browse through the rest of the parameters for [request](# "subtitle") to see if something else isrelevant to you. The rest of the parameters are used less frequently, often for special purposes,and they are documented in detail in the [§ 2.2.5 Requests](# "subtitle") section of this standard.

### Invoking fetch and processing responses

Aside from a [request](# "subtitle") the [fetch](# "subtitle") operation takes several optionalarguments. For those arguments that take an algorithm: the algorithm will be called from a task (orin a [parallel queue](# "subtitle") if
[*useParallelQueue*](# "subtitle") is true).

Once the [request](# "subtitle") is set up, to determine which algorithms to pass to[fetch](# "subtitle"), determine how you would like to process the [response](# "subtitle"), and inparticular at what stage you would like to receive a callback:
Upon completion          
This is how most callers handle a [response](# "subtitle"), for example  [scripts](# "subtitle") and [style resources](# "subtitle").  The [response](# "subtitle")’s [body](# "subtitle") is read in its entirety into a  [byte sequence](# "subtitle"), and then processed by the caller.  

To process a [response](# "subtitle") upon completion, pass an algorithm as the
[*processResponseConsumeBody*](# "subtitle") argument of [fetch](# "subtitle"). The given  algorithm is passed a [response](# "subtitle") and an argument representing the fully read  [body](# "subtitle") (of the [response](# "subtitle")’s  [internal response](# "subtitle")). The second argument’s values have the following  meaning:  
null         The [response](# "subtitle")’s [body](# "subtitle") is null, due to the response being a   [network error](# "subtitle") or having a [null body status](# "subtitle").        
failure         Attempting to [fully read](# "subtitle") the contents of the [response](# "subtitle")’s   [body](# "subtitle") failed, e.g., due to an I/O error.        
a [byte sequence](# "subtitle")                
[Fully reading](# "subtitle") the contents of the [response](# "subtitle")’s    [internal response](# "subtitle")’s [body](# "subtitle") succeeded.    

A [byte sequence](# "subtitle") containing the full contents will be passed also for a    [request](# "subtitle") whose [mode](# "subtitle") is
"`no-cors`". Callers have to    be careful when handling such content, as it should
not be accessible to the requesting    [origin](# "subtitle"). For example, the caller may use contents of a
"`no-cors`"    [response](# "subtitle") to display image contents directly to the user, but those image contents    should not be directly exposed to scripts in the embedding document.  


1.         Let request be a [request](# "subtitle") whose [URL](# "subtitle") is    `https://stuff.example.com/` and [client](# "subtitle") is [this](# "subtitle")’s    [relevant settings object](# "subtitle").    

2.         [Fetch](# "subtitle") request, with     [*processResponseConsumeBody*](# "subtitle") set to the following steps given     a [response](# "subtitle") response and null, failure, or a [byte sequence](# "subtitle")     contents:     


1.           If contents is null or failure, then present an error to the user.      

2.           Otherwise, parse contents considering the metadata from response,      and perform your own operations on it.     


Headers first, then chunk-by-chunk          
In some cases, for example when playing video or progressively loading images, callers
might  want to stream the response, and process it one chunk at a time. The [response](# "subtitle") is  handed over to the fetch caller once the headers are processed, and the caller  continues from there.  

To process a [response](# "subtitle") chunk-by-chunk, pass an algorithm to the  [*processResponse*](# "subtitle") argument of [fetch](# "subtitle"). The given  algorithm is passed a [response](# "subtitle") when the response’s headers have been  received and is responsible for reading the [response](# "subtitle")’s  [body](# "subtitle")’s [stream](# "subtitle") in order to download the rest  of the response. For convenience, you may also pass an algorithm to the
[*processResponseEndOfBody*](# "subtitle") argument, which is called once you have finished  fully reading the response and its [body](# "subtitle"). Note that unlike
[*processResponseConsumeBody*](# "subtitle"), passing the  [*processResponse*](# "subtitle") or
[*processResponseEndOfBody*](# "subtitle") arguments  does not guarantee that the response will be fully read, and callers are responsible to  read it themselves.  

The [*processResponse*](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") argument is also useful for handling the  [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [header list](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") and [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") without  handling the [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") at all. This is used, for example, when handling responses  that do not have an [ok status](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").  


1.         Let request be a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") whose [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is    `https://stream.example.com/` and [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is [this](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s    [relevant settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").    

2.         [Fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") request, with     [*processResponse*](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") set to the following steps given a     [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") response:     


1.           If response is a [network error](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), then present an error to the user.      

2.           Otherwise, if response’s [status](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") is not an      [ok status](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), present some fallback value to the user.      

3.           Otherwise, [get a reader](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") for [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s      [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle")’s [stream](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), and process in an appropriate way for the      MIME type identified by [extracting a MIME type](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") from response’s [headers list](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle").     


Ignore the response          
In some cases, there is no need for a [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") at all, e.g., in the case of  `[navigator.sendBeacon()](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg
"subtitle")`. Processing a response and passing callbacks to  [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is optional, so omitting the callback would [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") without  expecting a response. In such cases, the [response](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s  [stream](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") will be discarded, and the caller does not have to worry about downloading  the contents unnecessarily.  

[Fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") a [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") whose  [URL](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is
`https://fire-and-forget.example.com/`,  [method](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is
\``POST`\`, and [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is [this](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s  [relevant settings object](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").

Apart from the callbacks to handle responses, [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") accepts additional callbacksfor advanced cases.
[*processEarlyHintsResponse*](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is intended specifically for[responses](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") whose [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is 103, and is currently handled only bynavigations.
[*processRequestBodyChunkLength*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and[*processRequestEndOfBody*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") notify the caller of request body uploadingprogress.

Note that the [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") operation starts in the same thread from which it was called,and then breaks off to run its internal operations [in parallel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). The aforementioned callbacksare posted to a given [event loop](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") which is, by default, the[client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [global object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). To processresponses [in parallel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and handle interactions with the main thread by yourself,[fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with
[*useParallelQueue*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") set to true.

### Manipulating an ongoing fetch

To manipulate a [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") operation that has already started, use the[fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") returned by calling [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). For example, you may[abort](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") the [fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") due the user or page logic, or[terminate](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") it due to browser-internal circumstances.

In addition to terminating and aborting, callers may [report timing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")if this was not done automatically by passing the [initiator type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), or[extract full timing info](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and handle it on the caller side (this isdone only by navigations). The [fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is also used to[process the next manual redirect](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for [requests](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with[redirect mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") set to
"`manual`".

genLinkingSyntaxes(dfn) {    if(dfn.tagName != "DFN") return;    const type =
dfn.getAttribute('data-dfn-type');    if(!type) {        console.log(\`* doesn't
have a data-dfn-type:\`, dfn);        return [];    }    // Return a function that
wraps link text based on the type    const linkFormatter = linkFormatterFromType(type);
if(!linkFormatter) {        console.log(\`* has an unknown data-dfn-type:\`, dfn);
return [];    }    let ltAlts;    if(dfn.hasAttribute('data-lt')) {       
ltAlts = dfn.getAttribute('data-lt')            .split("|")            .map(x=>x.trim());
} else {        ltAlts = [dfn.textContent.trim()];    }    if(type == "type")
{        // lt of "", but "foo" is the interior;        // > is how you write it
with a for,        // not > or whatever.        for(var i = 0; i /.exec(lt);     
if(match) { ltAlts[i] = match[1]; }        }    }    let forAlts;   
if(dfn.hasAttribute('data-dfn-for')) {        forAlts = dfn.getAttribute('data-dfn-for')
.split(",")            .map(x=>x.trim());    } else {        forAlts =
[''];    }    let linkingSyntaxes = [];    if(!needsFor(type)) {        for(const
lt of ltAlts) {            linkingSyntaxes.push(linkFormatter(lt));        }    }
if(!refusesFor(type)) {        for(const f of forAlts) {           
linkingSyntaxes.push(linkFormatter(\`${f}/${ltAlts[0]}\`))        }    }    return
[        mk.b({}, 'Possible linking syntaxes:'),        mk.ul({},           
...linkingSyntaxes.map(link => {                const copyLink = async () =>    
await navigator.clipboard.writeText(link);                return
mk.li({},                    mk.div({ class: 'link-item' },                      
mk.button({                            class: 'copy-icon', title: 'Copy',      
type: 'button',                            _onclick: copyLink,
tabindex: 0,                        }, mk.span({ class:
'icon' }) ),                        mk.span({}, link)                    )       
);            })        )    ];}}
